W ARRANTSOFTRADE
Written and signed most often by the Emperor’s hand Himself, these Warrants were worth more than entire sub-sectors to those intelligent enough to 
understand them; though some among those handed these documents of authorisation understood that they were in effect also orders of banishment; 
permanent exile formalised and sealed with a wax crest.
DuringArmySelection,amodelwiththeWarrantsofTradeSpecialRulemustselectoneofthefollowingWarrants;thesemaygrantcertain
additionaleffectstotheDetachmentcontainingthemodelwiththisSpecialRule,aswellasaffectingwhichFactionsandSub-factionsmaybe
includedinthesamearmy.AmodelmayneverselectmorethanoneWarrantofTrade,andifamodelwiththeWarrantsofTradeSpecialRuleis
selectedaspartofanAlliedDetachmentthennoWarrantmaybeselected.
W ARRANTOFCOMPLIANCE
The most common Warrant in issue, the Warrant of Compliance was handed out in bulk to legitimise those who wished to enact personal campaigns to 
subdue star systems, enabling their brief but effective massacres to benefit those in command. Those issued these Warrants were often watched closely, though - 
and were not forgiven for mistakes made under the watchful eyes of their patrons. 
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademaynotincludeanyDetachmentsfromanyFactionwhichmayselecttheXenosAllegiance.
IfthearmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTradehastheLoyalistAllegiance,itmayincludeasingleAlliedDetachmentwiththeAgentsof
theEmperorFaction,butmaynotincludemorethanonesuchselection.
IfthearmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTradehastheTraitorAllegiance,itmayincludeasingleAlliedDetachmentwiththeAgentsof
theWarmasterFaction,butmaynotincludemorethanonesuchselection.
W ARRANTOFCONQUEST
The Warrant of Conquest was handed to those Rogue Traders who possessed a single-minded, bloody-handed nature; only those sought to carve out space for 
the Imperium to inhabit no matter the cost could truly bear such a writ. To this end, those issued this Warrant would enact great wars to reclaim systems for 
their patron - and were even willing to accept xenos aid to achieve their aims… 
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademayincludeasingleDetachmentfromanyFactionwhichmayselecttheXenosAllegiance,
butmaynotincludemorethanonesuchselection.
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademaynotincludeanyAlliedorOptionalDetachmentsfromtheAgentsoftheEmperoror
AgentsoftheWarmasterFactions.
W ARRANTOFEXPLORATION
Ranging far ahead of the main exploration fleet’s advances, those who bore a Warrant of Exploration were known for fraternising with the scattered human 
settlements they found, as well as the varied races of Xenos they encountered - and it was for this reason that the Legiones Astartes would often refuse to walk 
alongside them; though the denizens of the Mechanicum often did not hold such qualms…
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademayonlyincludeasingleDetachmentfromanyFactionwhichcontainsmodelswiththe
LegionesAstartes(X)SpecialRule.
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademayincludeuptotwoDetachmentsfromtheMechanicumFaction,butmaynotinclude
morethantwoofsuchselections.
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademustincludeaDetachmentfromanyFactionwhichmayselecttheXenosAllegiance,but
maynotincludemorethanonesuchselection.
ADetachmentcontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademaynotincludeanyAlliedorOptionalDetachmentsfromtheAgentsoftheEmperor
orAgentsoftheWarmasterFactions.
W ARRANT-BREAKER
Even so soon after their issue, tales abounded of those among the Traders Militant who could not remain within the terms of their Warrants; often, these 
Rogue Traders would find their Warrants declared null and void, and were set upon by loyalist and traitors both upon returning to Imperial space - to the 
detriment of those mortal souls they trailed with them to their doom.
AnarmycontainingamodelwiththisWarrantofTrademayonlyincludeAlliedorOptionalDetachmentsfromanyFactionwhichmayselectthe
XenosAllegiance,orwiththeImperialArmyFaction.ThesameFactionsorSub-factionsmaybeselectedintheseDetachments,butnoFactionor
Sub-factionmaybeselectedmorethantwice.ItshouldbenotedthatthisoverridesanyrestrictionsappliedtoselectingmorethanoneFactionor
Sub-factionwhichmayselecttheXenosAllegiance.
AllenemymodelsgainHatred(TradersMilitant)whenfightingagainstanarmythatincludesamodelwiththisWarrantofTrade.

## Page 9

Singular figures within the 
Imperium as well as without, 
the Rogue Traders were the 
Emperor’s attempt to solve 
two problems facing Him and 
His great design for 
mankind’s new domain; lack 
of resources and upstart 
rivals.
In issuing the documents 
known as Warrants of Trade, 
the Emperor legitimised those 
so invested with the right to 
stray outside both the borders 
and rules of the Imperium - 
For theirs was the way of 
negotiation, bartering, and 
compromise; and their 
Warrants permitted them to 
do so with not only human 
colonies not yet reintegrated 
into the Imperium, but also 
with the varied and 
multitudinous races of Xenos 
they encountered.
The individuals issued with 
Warrants were often as varied 
as the forces they commanded 
- whether raging egomaniacs, 
paranoid despots, ruthless 
privateers, or just and honest 
commanders, the common 
thread that linked them all 
was their dedication to their 
new life - and the acquisition 
of fame and fortune.
Not all Rogue Traders were so 
happy with their lot, however, 
and in those cases, Warrants 
could be quickly voided, their 
bearers named as traitors to 
the cause as a result. In these 
cases, the Trader Militant’s 
forces and their allies would 
find no peace or respite 
among the stars - for they 
would be hunted to the ends 
of the galaxy for daring to 
transgress against the 
Emperor Himself.

## Page 10

The Retainers Militant were 
those closest to the bearer of 
the Warrant of Trade, and in 
this trusted position they 
advised the Rogue Trader on 
all matters, warded their lives, 
and guarded their honour.
It was not uncommon for 
those among the factions that 
found themselves allied with 
the Traders Militant to send a 
representative to stand 
among such a cadre, allowing 
their concerns and 
motivations to be heard and 
understood directly - and 
ensuring the conditions of 
their support were upheld. 
Whilst occasionally 
uncomfortable in having to 
rub shoulders with the giants 
of the Astartes or Custodians, 
or the strange and varied 
forms of the Xenos, none 
among the retinue ever 
showed such weaknesses 
openly; for theirs was the way 
of alliances and promises, 
careful negotiations, and the 
weaving of friendships for 
mutual gain.
16

JOKAERO COURTIER
The strange creatures known 
as Jokaero were an 
uncommon sight across the 
nascent Imperium - for in 
truth, they represented one of 
the very few Xenos races 
openly tolerated by humanity 
- and actively sought out by a 
few. Jokaero, whilst 
animalistic and close in form 
to an ancient Terran simian, 
were seemingly remarkably 
intelligent despite lacking the 
ability to speak, having an 
innate skill with technology 
that outstripped their physical 
appearance. Anything left 
long enough in their presence 
would eventually be tinkered 
with, modified to fit the 
creature’s whims - though it 
was just as likely the 
modifications would do 
nothing as it was to make the 
object in question incredibly 
potent in its uses.
The Jokaero themselves often 
were surrounded by small, 
hovering metallic orbs which 
projected energy fields to 
defend them whenever the 
creature perceived a threat, 
whilst their long and spindly 
fingers were often replaced 
with metallic armatures that 
contained miniaturised 
weapons of unbelievable 
potency.
Rogue Traders often enjoyed 
the company of such 
creatures, bartering food and 
shelter to them in return for 
the Jokaero’s continued 
presence - as well as knowing 
that anything ‘accidentally’ 
left in their presence for a 
while might become 
immensely more useful… 
HQ
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
JokaeroCourtier 6 1 3 2 3 1 3 1 7 -
The medicae attached to the 
fleets of the Traders Militant 
were often paid handsomely 
for exclusive, life-long service 
to the Trader Militant cause, 
and equipped as such with the 
very best equipment - paid for 
from the Rogue Trader’s own 
pocket - to ensure they were 
effective in their duties.
Often it would be required for 
the Adjuevants to undergo 
extensive training in the arts 
of rejuvenat treatments, 
allowing the Rogue Trader 
and those they deemed worthy 
to extend their voyages for 
decades beyond human 
capacities, living lives of a 
length far beyond ordinary 
human ken. 
18

The individuals known as 
Templax Challengers were a 
diverse and unusual group; 
comprised as often of high-
born nobles with something 
to prove as much as they were 
hiver scum with a grudge to 
settle. These varied beginnings 
aside, however, the singular 
thread that linked these 
disparate individuals into a 
single classification was their 
consummate skill with a 
blade. 
No matter the case of their 
upbringing, these individuals 
would swear bonds of fealty 
to a Trader Militant - perhaps 
hoping to earn favour or 
fortune - and would thusly be 
expected to fight in defence of 
their lives, bound by their 
honour to take on any who 
offered insult or injury to their 
liege lord no matter the risk to 
their own life. Armed and 
equipped to complete their 
duties from the Rogue 
Trader’s own supplies and 
pockets, the legends these 
individuals would weave in 
service to their lieges would 
live beyond their mortal 
lifespan.
HQ
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
TemplaxChallenger 6 6 4 3 3 2 5 3 8 4+
The Navigator households 
were not ignorant to the 
needs of the wider Crusade, 
and many found themselves 
requested to provide 
assistance to the mustering 
expeditionary fleets. Some 
saw this as a perfect 
opportunity to rid themselves 
of ascendant members of the 
households perceived to be a 
threat in times ahead - and 
so, many Navigators assigned 
to the far-flung Rogue Trader 
fleets would come to realise 
their assignment was as much 
to fulfil the obligations to the 
Emperor as it was to get rid of 
their troublesome selves.

Whilst generally a broad 
classification, the sanctioned 
psykers known as Psykana 
were a tolerated transgression 
against the Emperor’s will 
that wielded psychic powers 
openly, trained in the 
methods of doing so safely in 
order to bolster the ranks of 
the Imperium’s armies against 
the poorly understood 
sorcerous threats of the Great 
Crusade. The most powerful 
of their number were known 
as Primus Psykana, and were 
able to pour forth great bolts 
of esoteric lightning, gelid 
flame, and even more unusual 
and abhorrent power.
Among the fleets bound to 
Rogue Traders, whilst rare, 
there were still accounts of 
Primus Psykana being 
unleashed in battle at the will 
of their masters; their deadly 
and arcane powers being put 
to seeming good use against 
foes with unknowable power 
of their own.
HQ
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
PrimusPsykana 6 4 4 3 3 2 3 2 8 4+
The Astropaths bound to a 
Trader Militant fleet were 
often selected specifically for 
their abilities above and 
beyond the more ordinary of 
their kin; able to project clear 
messages in comparison to 
others of their order and 
skilled in diplomacy in ways 
others not of their order could 
not hope to manage. Indeed, 
as advisors and confidants 
many found their place at the 
right hand of the Rogue 
Traders themselves, able to 
enjoy the comforts of such a 
life despite the solitude a 
psyker might ordinarily 
expect.

*A Legion Presage that selects a Boarding Shield also gains the Heavy Sub-type.
Hailing from the serried ranks 
of the Legiones Astartes, those 
commanders seconded to the 
Rogue Trader courts as 
representatives were known as 
Presages. In this role they were 
expected to safeguard the 
interests of the Legion during 
their duties, as well as the 
honour of the Legion itself. To 
invite dishonour upon the 
Astartes was accepted by most 
mortals who had witnessed 
them in battle to be a death 
sentence; as a result, the 
presence of a Presage would 
still the tongue of even the 
most outspoken of a Trader 
Militant’s courtiers.
Despite their relatively 
pedestrian assignment, 
however, a Presage was still a 
member of the Legiones 
Astartes, and a high-ranking 
one at that - and so whilst 
consummate skill with their 
weapons was expected as the 
default, their wit and insight 
was often rather more 
unexpected of them - though 
all who treated with them 
agreed their tongues could be 
just as sharp, if not more so, 
than their blades.
HQ
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
LegionPresage 7 5 5 4 4 2 5 3 9 2+
Defensor-Automata were a 
common sight in the retinues 
of the most paranoid and 
reclusive Rogue Traders, who 
purchased their augmented 
charges from Mechanicum 
Forge Worlds with which they 
had relatively good standing. 
Their immense bulk, 
resistance to pain, and ability 
to bear heavier armaments 
than most humans could 
manage was well liked and 
respected by many in the 
Trader Militant - and none 
could deny their effectiveness 
having witnessed them in 
battle - though the primary 
attraction to them was often 
joked to be their inability to 
refuse orders.

Those from among the 
massed ranks of human 
soldiers who formed the most 
numerous of the armies of the 
Great Crusade who fought 
alongside the Traders 
Militant were known as the 
Militaris Adjutants. 
Responsible for the 
provisioning of ammunition, 
food, and all the other 
materiel needed for the 
mortal elements of a fleet to 
stage a campaign, Adjutants 
had to be both diplomatic 
and ruthless beyond even the 
other elements of a Rogue 
Trader’s entourage; for it was 
all too easy for an Astartes or 
Custodian to dismiss the 
concerns of an unmodified 
human, and so fighting their 
corner was a required skill.
For their bravery - their 
single-minded willingness to 
stomp down upon the 
Transhuman Dread that 
bubbled from within when 
faced with one of the 
Emperor’s sons and speak 
their minds or fight for their 
lives - did these officers earn 
the grudging respect of all.
HQ

Hailing from the forges of 
Mechanicum, the Magos 
Attendis were responsible for 
upholding the laws of their 
holy order during the ranging 
missions performed by the 
Traders Militant fleets. In this 
role, many different castes 
and sub-classifications of the 
Taghmatas and Divisios were 
represented - including both 
the Legio Titanicus and the 
Questoris Households, as well 
as ever more obscure sub-
divisions and doctrines of the 
Martian Priesthood. No 
matter their origin, these 
magi were expected to assist 
in matters of both their cult’s 
doctrinal purity - and in 
matters of war, co-ordinating 
the deployment of their 
engines of destruction as 
requested by the Rogue 
Trader.

Executors of the Black Fleets 
held a sinister and dubious 
prestige among the Rogue 
Traders, for the duties that 
required standing shoulder-
to-shoulder with such 
ominous figures were often 
rumoured to be some of the 
most important or critical to 
the survival of the nascent 
Imperium, and so to be tasked 
to them was considered an 
honour of sorts.
Among the Silent Sisterhood 
themselves, no such feeling 
was present. Regarding their 
assignments to the Traders 
Militant as an irritation and 
a distraction, often many of 
them would simply slog 
through the time their forces 
spent bound, desperate to be 
released from their shared 
service to once again pursue 
their highest calling in the 
hunting of the Mutant and 
the Pskyer.
Those Sisters given the task of 
representing their warhost in 
the court of the Rogue Traders 
were often noted to be 
particularly powerful blanks; 
ones who could both hide 
their true nature as nulls to 
assuage the horror inspired by 
their presence, or otherwise go 
in the opposite direction and 
amplifying the effects of their 
blank-souled nature to cause 
discomfort or even outright 
pain in those foolish or 
unlucky enough to irritate 
them.
HQ
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
ExecutoroftheBlackFleet6 5 5 3 3 2 5 3 9 2+
Assigned often at the 
instruction of the Emperor 
Himself, the Custodians 
known as Observers were not 
a specific rank, but instead a 
duty - instructed to watch for 
signs that a fledgling Rogue 
Trader was correctly utilising 
their Warrant of Trade and 
that their investiture as such 
was not a mistake or a waste.
In this duty, the Custodian so 
appointed would remain 
steadfastly committed - 
though it was not entirely 
unknown, over the long 
periods of their assignment, 
for a Custodian to become 
somewhat attached to their 
mortal charge, sharing a bond 
that could even be taken by 
outsiders for something 
approaching friendship.

Where the representatives of 
other groups, or even races in 
the case of the Xenos, were 
distinctive and varied, the 
Faceless Delegates of the 
Saedathii were all alike in 
every way. From the 
colourations of their cloaks, to 
their soft-spoken lilting voices, 
and especially the facelessly 
blank masks they wore, no 
Delegate was ever distinctive 
enough to pick apart, and it 
was often whispered one 
would change place with 
another at random to try and 
unnerve or wrong-foot the 
Rogue Traders who consorted 
with them. Despite these 
theatrics, the Faceless 
Delegates were always fair 
and amicable, offering terms 
that seemed generous to all 
who heard them and 
reasonable even to the 
standards of the most 
demanding negotiators - 
though to what ends the 
Harlequins were playing, 
none could ever tell…

Tolerated with barely-
suppressed contempt by some 
within the Traders Militant, 
but welcomed with open arms 
and warm smiles by others, 
the Envoys of the Asuryani 
were representatives of the 
great Aeldari Craftworlds, 
tasked with ensuring that any 
alliance between human and 
Eldar was at the least 
beneficial to at least the Asur 
and Traders Militant - if not 
the wider Imperium.
Despite the obvious mistrust 
from some, or the doubts of 
falsehood in others, many 
such alliances were witnessed 
- for the list of allies available 
to the Asur grew thin during 
the waning years of the Great 
Crusade, and many 
understood it would more 
conducive to their continued 
survival to ally themselves 
with the burgeoning 
Imperium.
Unbeknownst to both sides of 
these uneasy alliances, the 
horrors of Horus’ war upon 
the galaxy would soon reveal 
the folly of such logic.
30

## Page 19

Recruited from among the 
ranks of the Solar Auxilia and 
other prestigious regiments of 
the Imperial Army - or 
otherwise drawn from private 
militaries and protectorates - 
the Voidsmen of the Traders 
Militant were usually 
employed in the role of 
protecting the interests of the 
Rogue Trader and their 
entourage. Well-armed and 
equipped, the loyalty 
displayed by Voidsmen was 
proof that such a thing could 
be bought - for the right price.
The best men and women 
from among the Voidsmen 
were often recruited into other 
functions in the Trader 
Militant’s forces - whether to 
pilot Grav-Attacks, 
Carnodons, or transports, or 
otherwise to join the ranks of 
the Savaris or Imperatrix; any 
among them who showed skill 
was surely rewarded, in an 
effort not only to field the best 
possible forces, but to keep 
their purchased loyalty firmly 
and truly in place.

Among those bondsmen who 
excelled in the art of ranged 
combat, there was often the 
chance for promotion to an 
Imperatrix Cadre. On the 
battlefield, the Imperatrix 
were expected to provide 
covering fire for their 
employers should their deals 
go awry. A prospective 
Imperatris would be drilled in 
the skills required for precision 
under pressure, as well as the 
methods and skills required to 
maintain the weapons they 
were assigned.
Once their training was 
complete, the Imperatris 
would be formally invested 
into a corps, and equipped 
with their choice from a 
maddening array of man-
portable ranged equipment - 
ranging from needle to volkite 
weaponry, and including 
everything between.

## Page 20

37
Where the Imperatrix 
Bondsmen Cadres were 
composed of those individuals 
raised from the Voidsmen who 
excelled in ranged combat, the 
Savaris Cadres were instead 
entirely formed of those 
Voidsmen who were most at 
home in the swirling 
maelstrom of bloody melee. 
In combat, the Savaris were 
well known for savagery - 
contrasting the orderly rank 
and file of the Imperatrix, or 
the cautious masses of 
Voidsmen - and were often 
witnessed charging into battle 
with shouted obscenities and 
chats; marking themselves as 
the veterans of a thousand 
wars, kept in fighting 
condition by a mix of rejuvant 
treatments and stimms.

The Arvus Lighter was a 
common and mass-produced 
transport that was within 
reach and budget for almost 
all branches of the Imperial 
military - as well as for the 
private armies of planetary 
governors, Auxilia 
commanders, and Rogue 
Traders. A venerable design 
that had been in use since 
before the dawn of the Great 
Crusade, the Arvus was well 
known for being easy to repair 
and refit, a quirk that ensured 
it remained popular despite 
other more advanced models 
being introduced toward the 
Crusade’s end.

Comprising a robust, modular 
chassis, and an armoured 
crew compartment big 
enough to seat twelve 
passengers, the Aurox 
Transport was a relatively 
recent innovation, designed 
and produced in bulk at the 
commencement of the 
Crusade to enable the massed 
ranks of the Imperial Army to 
be transported without the 
comparatively high 
expenditures required to 
produce the Rhino 
Transporter chassis.
These massive production 
quantities inevitably resulted 
in surplus vehicles, which were 
left behind as the grinding 
advance of the Great Crusade 
pushed onward - and these 
were eagerly taken up by 
many, including the forces of 
the Traders Militant, allowing 
them a cheap and reliable 
mode of traversing alien 
worlds.

Only the most fantastically 
wealthy Rogue Traders could 
afford the statement of 
absolute authority that was a 
Land Raider Armoured 
Carrier - the immense size 
and complexity in 
constructing them ensuring 
they commanded a high price 
from any Magos willing to 
entertain the concept of 
selling one to the Trader 
Militant in the first place. Yet, 
despite the prohibitive cost 
involved, some Rogue Traders 
owned not one of these 
powerful vehicles but several, 
using them to ensure that any 
who took to the field at their 
command was properly 
protected in order to allow 
them to properly fulfil their 
duties.
Thanks to their high cost, 
however, it was common for 
the Land Raiders so 
purchased to be customised 
further to ensure their 
purchase could properly 
defend itself; various 
configurations proved popular 
over the years of the Great 
Crusade, with the ever-
common Lascannon and 
Heavy Bolter combination 
oft-seen - though everything 
from Melta to Volkite 
loadouts were recorded as 
having been witnessed in 
battle.

## Page 25

The Grav-Attack was a finicky 
and awkward piece of 
equipment that proved 
annoyingly difficult for many 
to master, in spite of its 
incredible top speed and 
ability to bear a pilot to 
unusually high altitudes for 
an anti-grav vehicle. In being 
unable to support the weight 
of a fully-armoured Astartes, 
it also never found favour 
amongst the Legiones - and 
this lack of favour would have 
led to its extinction, if it were 
not for the diligent work of 
the Forge World of Arkadia, 
alongside the Traders 
Militant.
Refining the chassis to include 
stabilisation fins, and to use 
the weight of multiple items 
of heavy armament to 
balance the grav-attack itself, 
allowed the craft to finally see 
active use - although solely 
within the purview of the 
Rogue Trader’s forces 
themselves.

The Carnodon was being 
largely put aside within the 
Imperium toward the end of 
the Great Crusade, finding 
itself replaced by the Leman 
Russ for a variety of reasons 
in many forces. One place 
where the Carnodon was not 
just maintained, but 
preferred, however, was the 
forces of the Traders Militant 
- where the increased speed 
and manoeuvrability of the 
chassis had earned it the 
favour of all who crewed 
them.
Used in formations known as 
‘Severax’, these Carnodon 
squadrons would separate 
after rolling from their 
landers, peeling off into 
distinct groups to encircle and 
harass the enemy to allow the 
lighter elements of the Rogue 
Trader’s forces to advance 
under their enfilading 
crossfire.

CHAMBERSOFTHE GOLDENHOST
Throughout the whole of the Unification Wars and most of the early Great Crusade, the forces of the Legio Custodes were not widespread nor commonly seen 
among the nascent Imperium. Tasked solely with protecting the Emperor and enacting His will, they were only found en-mass when their ranks surrounded 
their ward. While smaller sodalities and even individual warriors were often tasked with highly specific missions such as watching over the Primarchs, the 
onset of the Heresy proved the folly of this method of deployment. As the fires of war engulfed the galaxy once more, the peerless warriors of the Legio Custodes 
assembled into their mighty Shield-Hosts to deny Horus’ ascendancy once and for all.

CHAMBEROFTHE GOLDENHOST:HYKANATOI
The Hykanatoi made up the bulk of the Legio Custodes, heavy infantry clad in Auramite armour that effortlessly crushed all resistance against them. Most 
were armed with the iconic Guardian Spear and Misericordia, though this chamber also held the defensive specialists of the Sentinel Guard and the Emperor’s 
three-hundred Companions, the Hetaeron Guard. Taken together, the Hykanatoi represented the absolute authority of the Emperor’s will.

Tharanatoi exemplified the unbridled strength of the Emperor’s wrath.

SHIELD-HOSTSOFTHE LEGIOCUSTODES
Among the Legio Custodes, many distinctive and recognisable differences in how different Shield-Hosts waged war existed, even before the outbreak of the 
Horus Heresy. These Shield-Hosts would often take immense pride in their unique methods of war; seeing their prowess and traditions as their own personal 
dedication to the Emperor made manifest. Foremost in the Legio Custodes in shaping and maintaining these traditions were the Host-Masters, and when an 
army of Custodians sallied forth in His name, they often included these representatives of a Shield-Host.

AQUILANSHIELD
Among the Legio Custodes, there were some who were charged not with defending the Emperor, but instead those deemed vitally important by either His 
unknowable will, or that of Malcador’s design. The Aquilan Shield undertook these protective duties with stoic determination, their protection often as 
unexpected as it was unwavering.

Masters of the lightning assault, shock and awe strikes, and overwhelming power, the Dread Host took sole responsibility for delivering the hammer-blow of

could stand against their might.

The white-armoured castellans of the Solar Watch held their sacred duty to monitor the Sol system as a singular honour. Tasked as the first line of defence

SHADOW KEEPERS
Only the custodians of truly singular will, even among their exemplary order, were recruited to the ranks of the Shadow Keepers; tasked to maintain and

foes, either living or dead - though many of their enemies would sooner die than be taken alive...

Captains excelled at leading 
charges on foot or otherwise 
directing the flow of battle 
from the swirling maelstrom 
of combat, a small few 
preferred the harrying and 
flanking manoeuvres afforded 
by the power of a Jetbike. 
Those who excelled at 
directing war from their 
steeds, and were proficient 
enough to bring low the 
enemy’s elite formations in 
the doing so, were monikered 
the ‘Vertus Praetorians’ - 
though upon those Shield 
Captains who accepted such a 
duty, the name ‘Vertoii’ was 
commonly added to their rote 
of names as they enacted their 
deeds.

A relatively new invention 
before the dawning of the 
Heresy, Allarus Terminator 
Armour nevertheless had both 
its detractors and its 
proponents among the Legio 
Custodes. Some praised its 
retained speed and dexterity 
despite the improved 
protection it offered a wearer, 
whilst some scorned it as 
lesser than the mighty 
Aquilon suits. Despite this, 
Allarus Armour was seen in 
greater numbers by the days 
of the siege, its inbuilt 
targeting systems allowing the 
Custodians who wore it to 
engage in acts of headhunting 
to attempt to blunt the traitor 
offensives.
Following such successes, 
none among the ranks of the 
Custodians could doubt the 
armour’s effectiveness when 
worn by a skilled leader - and 
the results it offered in defence 
of the scions of the Emperor 
were beyond doubt.

A rare breed among the Legio 
Custodes, those Shield 
Captains regarded as Aquilius 
were content to move at a far 
slower pace than that of their 
brethren. Not for them the 
lightning assault, but instead 
the ponderous and slow grind 
of Aquilon Terminator 
Armour, and the grim finality 
that came with the pounding 
footfalls of armoured suits 
that out-weighed some battle 
tanks. 
Making use of this weighty, 
somewhat unwieldy, 
advantage was a skill few 
among the Custodians could 
master; yet those of the 
Tharanatoi who did so would 
go to war clad in armoured 
plate that rendered their 
forms as golden statues of 
lore.

For some Shield Captains, 
time spent among their 
charges could often result in 
idiosyncrasies in fighting style 
despite the singular way in 
which they were normally 
witnessed to make war. In 
these instances, some would 
opt to take up the wargear in 
which they felt best suited 
their preferences; and so it 
could sometimes be witnessed 
that a Shield Captain 
descended from on high, like 
some golden-armoured vision 
of an angel from old Terran 
myth.

## Page 36

Where the mighty Aquilon 
pattern Terminator Armour 
was an evolution of the 
Cataphractii armour used by 
the Legiones Astartes, the 
Allarus pattern was a 
furthering of the Tartaros 
pattern, and nowhere was this 
more evident than in the 
manoeuvrability it granted its 
Custodian charges.
In this extra mobility came a 
unique duty and usage for 
those Custodians who took up 
its use; the ordained duty as 
the hunters of men, the slayers 
of kings; the killers of the 
Emperor Himself, beholden to 
His needs alone to remove a 
foe’s command structure to 
allow their golden-armoured 
brothers to bring low the 
enemy.

As the horrors of Horus’ 
betrayal and the scale of the 
destruction he unleashed were 
revealed upon the galaxy, not 
even the peerless warriors of 
the Legio Custodes could hope 
to avoid the spiralling cost of 
lives. To this end, as ever more 
Custodes fell in battle, the 
Chamber of the Honoured 
Dead Who Walked, the 
Moritoi, proffered an answer; 
and so, upon the battlefields 
of sunless realms, across dead 
moons, and even on the 
surface of Terra itself, did 
Contemptor Dreadnoughts 
retrofitted with advanced 
technology held only by the 
Emperor and His most trusted 
allies walk.

If the Custodians who rode 
the Agamatus Jetbikes into 
battle were the mighty 
lancer’s charge, the ones who 
rode the Dawneagle jetbikes 
were the scouts, aimed to 
harry and punish the foe from 
an unexpected quarter. Slower 
than the Agamatus, the 
Dawneagles relied upon 
manoeuvrability and the 
power of their mounts to do 
damage - as well as their 
specially-designed lances, 
which were far more apt for 
shredding their foes in 
brutally effective charges that 
none could hope to easily 
defend against.

Unlike the more common 
Land Raider, the Legio 
Custodes retained 
near-unique access to an 
anti-gravity equipped variant. 
Dubbed the ‘Grav-Raider’ - 
much to Arkhan Land’s 
annoyance - this variant 
chassis was uncommon even 
before the Heresy began, 
requiring much material and 
toil to manufacture even a 
single example. It was also 
slower, thanks to the extra 
weight required for the 
gravity-repellers it utilised to 
remain afloat; a factor that 
limited its utility for those 
among the Custodes who 
required rapid transportation 
to the front lines.

Whilst many factions in the 
Age of Darkness utilised the 
Land Raider chassis in various 
forms and formats, none did 
so in quite an exemplary 
manner as the Legio 
Custodes. As befitted their 
high station, their Land 
Raiders were often retrofitted 
with various weaponry not 
available to any outside of 
their elevated brotherhoods, 
whilst the chassis themselves 
were of the finest make; 
wargear often only seen in 
scant numbers common to 
their frames. It was upon such 
peerless transports that the 
Custodians of the Emperor 
made war - and in the doing 
so did they set the patterns in 
which others would merely 
ape in their use of the Land 
Raider in war.

Requiring even greater 
expenditure and effort to 
produce and maintain than a 
standard Spartan Assault 
Tank and a Grav-Raider 
combined, the Grav-Spartan, 
as it was commonly known, 
was a behemoth seen so rarely 
upon the battlefields of the 
Great Crusade that it was 
often rumoured to not exist; a 
butt of jokes told by Astartes 
and mortals alike.
The sight of such an immense 
craft hovering cleanly a few 
feet above the turmoil of 
churned earth, shell and shot, 
and more that composed 
most battle-grounds oft killed 
the humour dead.
The colossal bulk of such 
vehicles, coupled with the 
powerful anti-gravitic 
repulsion effect they 
generated, inspired awe and 
dread in those who witnessed 
their approach - a fitting 
chariot for the guardians of 
Mankind.

Where the Legiones Astartes 
war-machine would bring a 
Land Raider Spartan to bear 
only for their most skilled 
warriors to ride to war within, 
the Legio Custodes held no 
such qualms about their use, 
allowing any who required 
swift passage upon the field of 
battle to make use of their 
thickly armoured hulls. 
Indeed, thanks to the sheer 
size and scale of such a 
vehicle, the Spartan Assault 
Tank was one of only a 
handful of vehicles which 
could successfully transport 
larger squads of the Emperor’s 
elite, safe from the myriad 
firepower raining down upon 
the outer ceramite skin of 
their steed.

## Page 48

Knight Sikari Dar had fought 
and bled as one of the Silent 
Sisterhood almost since its 
inauguration, and by the time 
the Great Crusade's end 
neared her face was lined and 
worn with the weight of her 
duty to the cause. A war-
leader by her actions, Sikari 
had chosen to allow others to 
lead the Sisterhood from the 
higher echelons of power - for 
her leadership required only to 
be at the head of a charge, a 
vicious but noiseless snarl 
upon her lips, as her immense 
polearm Nightfall ripped 
through the flesh of her 
enemy.
Indeed, the strength possessed 
by Dar was famed by many 
within and without the ranks 
of the Null-Maidens - for even 
some Custodians offered her 
genuine respect, seeing in her 
a strength that did not exist 
in most mortals. This respect 
was well-earned; the tales of 
her besting xenos war-beasts 
and warlords both in single 
combat were well-known, 
even outside the signed-and-
signalled speech of the Sisters, 
but in the dawning of Horus' 
war upon the Imperium and 
the coming of the benighted 
horrors of the warp was an 
even greater prey for her to 
claim her victory over; the 
fel-daemons of the Great 
Enemy her greatest and truest 
foes. Into such a crucible 
would a lesser being have 
simply capitulated, 
overwhelmed by the sheer 
malice of such an enemy - 
and yet, in such battles did 
Sikari Dar thrive.
92

Vigilance, Knight Ensigns 
were inspiring emissaries and 
warriors born under the

were also made to bare blade

and act as a figurehead for 
their fellows to rally around in

charges rousing valour and 
fervour around the Emperor’s

Where the Eradicator Cadres 
of the Chamber of Vigilance 
were one of the Order’s many 
hammers, the rare Indictor 
Cadres acted as the anvil. An 
unopposed wall of veterans, 
they imposed their vigil-watch 
aboard the black ships, 
wrangling the most 
uncooperative in the name of 
the Great Tithe; called upon 
to form an indomitable 
spearhead in the field against 
psyker incursions.
The voidscale plate in which 
they were clad made use of 
technologies borne from both 
Selenite void-mail and 
astartes power armour; 
although it also incorporated 
advanced technologies gained 
through the long development 
of Tactical Dreadnought 
Armour. This vastly improved 
protection had the side effect 
of slowing the wearer - but 
not reducing their overall 
manoeuvrability, and 
allowing the Indictor Cadres 
to act as a bastion of 
nullifying power.

Bearers of esoteric and 
forbidden weapons deployed 
only in the most dire of times, 
Perditor Cadres were 
assemblies of Silent Sisters 
who bore the mournful 
honour of taking those dread 
weapons to battle. 
By the Knight-Commander’s 
command, license to use such 
horrific devices against the 
Imperium’s foes was granted. 
Mass-reactive Null 
armaments and esoteric 
beams of molecular 
disruption were but a few 
weapons borne into battle in 
the hands of the Perditors - 
the Oblivion Chamber 
making use of these accursed 
weapons to decisively 
eradicate any foe they faced.

## Page 52

As the Crusade raged on, the 
tithe-fleets of the Sisterhood 
began to meet harsher 
resistance from both 
psychically-skilled Xenos and 
human colonies both. To 
ensure their continued ability 
to enact the tithes without 
throwing the lives of the ever-
rare Null-Maidens away, the 
Emperor commanded in 
secret that the Mechanicum 
assist Him in finding a 
solution.
The end result of this 
collaboration were hulking 
automata which could 
capture those empowered 
with arcane abilities thanks to 
the protection offered by the 
interworked wards and 
intricate psy-circuitry within 
their armour. In secret, 
however, a third factor tied 
this unholy miracle together - 
for at their core lay an 
enigma; the Mechanicum had 
constructed a system by which 
they could seal the very mind 
of one of the Sisterhood who 
had perished, ensuring that 
even in death, they would not 
know peace - their very 
essence reforged into 
something akin to the 
automata which the Martian 
Priesthood so venerated.
Knowledge of this 
bastardisation of the minds of 
their kin was entrusted only 
to a scant handful of Sisters, 
and though they were 
disgusted at the truth, the 
Emperor Himself had 
approved the Automata’s use - 
and so, the Mistresses of the 
Repressors were taught to 
maintain and to safeguard 
the machines, prosecuting the 
Emperor’s will in both the 
Crusade - and later, in the 
darkened days of the Heresy.
100

As the Silent Sisterhood 
carried out their ordained 
duties in the name of the 
Emperor during the Great 
Crusade, it became 
increasingly obvious that 
material shortages were 
hampering their efforts. Chief 
among this was a lack of 
transportation vehicles for 
their chambers - for Aquisitors 
were expensive to produce 
and difficult to maintain at 
the best of times, but at the 
far end of an advancing 
crusade fleet this issue was 
exacerbated tenfold. To 
remedy this, the Silent 
Sisterhood began 
experimenting with the use of 
Rhino Transports.
Refitting a Rhino to carry 
twelve Sisters was an easy 
affair, as was the task of 
learning how to control such 
a vehicle - and so the effort to 
introduce the Rhino was 
deemed an immediate success, 
and all Chambers found 
themselves with a stockpile of 
the vehicles within scant years 
of the program’s approval.
104

Unique among the Rhino 
Transport variants found 
across the Imperium, the 
Grav-Rhino was a rare 
variant of the ubiquitous 
vehicle found in every 
Legiones Astartes Armoury - 
and in that of many mortal 
armies too. Fitted with serried 
rows of repulsor plates, these 
vehicles were capable of a 
limited kind of flight - and 
could traverse almost any 
kind of terrain with ease.
Often used to ferry squads of 
Silent Sisters to their targets 
in locations where a Kharon 
Pattern Aquisitor would not 
be practical, by the time of the 
dark days of the Horus Heresy 
the Grav-Rhino was an 
uncommon sight on the 
battlefields visited by the 
Sisterhood - but nevertheless 
one recognisable as a mark of 
their presence.

Shrikes were the rapid-
response and reconnaissance 
spearhead of the Chamber of 
Oblivion. Fast, even for the 
Sisters of Silence, the Shrikes 
wore chainmail of selenite 
origin; often wielding twin 
blades coated in an ichor 
which disrupted and distorted 
the very fabric of space. To 
those unattuned, this was a 
dangerous toxin unlike any 
other - but to those who held 
even a sliver of aetheric power 
it could cause catastrophic 
and often fatal repercussions.
Shrikes were rarely seen, in 
part due to their null-soul, but 
also due to their alacrity. 
Hopping from cover to cover 
in an unseen blur to the 
mortal eye, they would close 
on key targets and - like the 
Terran bird of ages past - 
swoop down to butcher the 
foe; irregardless of their origin 
in either real-space or the 
immaterium.

A harrowing mirror to the 
ethereal nature of the Erinyes 
Jetbikes, Impetor Cadres were 
near silent in their running. 
Taking to the field on 
elegantly crafted all-terrain 
Alkonost Bikes, these mounts 
allowed the Impetors to 
advance silently before 
delivering lightning-hard 
strikes with their lances, 
delivering bursts of actinic 
energy to engulf unsuspecting 
foes. Mounted firearms on 
their mount’s sides allowed 
the Sisters to harass targets 
from afar as they sought the 
perfect opening on their next 
target; cycling their charges to 
deliver continuous strikes 
from their weapons.

Where the Xiphon Interceptor 
flown by the Legiones Astartes 
was a well-known sight above 
the battlefields of the Great 
Crusade, as well as the Age of 
Darkness that followed, the 
Aymhari was a far more 
elusive and unknown beast. 
Sharing a technological base 
with the Xiphon, the Aymhari 
was a descendent of the 
Amhut Voyager; bearing 
strange and fanciful wing-
vanes with bladed edges to 
better manoeuvre at immense 
speeds.
Where the Xiphon required 
either an cybernetically-
enhanced human pilot - or 
one of the trans-human 
Legionaries - to master its 
incredible speed and agility, 
the Aymhari circumvented 
this need by instead invasively 
linking into its pilot’s mind 
directly via an MIU, 
maintaining and stabilising 
their conscious mind, whilst 
automated systems monitored 
and dumped quantities of 
stimulant and aero-active 
drugs as required directly into 
the pilot’s bloodstream to 
ensure they remained intact 
despite the gravitational and 
inertial forces applied to their 
mortal bodies.
The invasive nature of the 
craft’s control mechanisms, as 
well as the complexity of its 
construction, played a large 
hand in all-but-guaranteeing 
its exclusive adoption by only 
the Silent Sisterhoods.

Recruited from among the 
ranks of the Oblivion Knights, 
the pilots of Caedecii exo-
frames were well known for 
being some of the most 
aggressive and unforgiving 
members of the Silent Sisters, 
and it was to this end that 
they were entrusted with some 
of the heaviest weaponry their 
order could bring to bear.
The exo-frames with which 
they were equipped were 
themselves masterworks from 
a time far before the dawning 
of the Imperium - armoured 
suits granting their wearer 
both immense strength and 
resilience, whilst not 
hindering their fighting 
ability. This came at a high 
cost, however - for the suits 
were rare beyond measure, 
and maintaining them was a 
task that even the minds of 
the Mechanicum’s best 
artificers were frequently 
puzzled by.
Whilst the delicate 
mechanical internals of the 
suits themselves were often 
hard to decipher, one more 
modern addition the Emperor 
and Malcador themselves had 
sanctioned was the power of 
the Sinistrae Magnificat - 
prototypes of the weapons 
found on the dread Ordo 
Sinister titans, capable of 
ripping at the very souls of the 
unfortunate psykers who 
found themselves treated as 
mere batteries to power their 
dread mechanisms.

SOLARAUXILIAW ARLORDTRAITS
The warlords of the Solar Auxilia were incredibly varied, united only by two factors - rank and skill. In this regard, no matter their preferred battlefields or 
specialisations, it was well-known among those who faced the serried ranks of the Auxilia on the field that to underestimate these mortal commanders was to 
court destruction and death.
IfyourWarlordisoftheSolarAuxiliaSub-faction,insteadofchoosingaWarlordTraitfromtheCoreRulebook’sWarlordTraits,youmayinstead
chooseaWarlordTraitfromtheoptionsbelow.
SOLARAUXILIAW ARLORDTRAIT:VOIDM ASTER
Tasked with exploring the harshest the galaxy has to offer, from the Galactic core to the furthest edges of the frontier, some commanders had fought in 
environments which would have broken lesser officers. From leading firefights in ever-shifting gravity, co-ordinating armoured warfare in gas so thick it slowed 
movement, and sieging fortresses of foul xenos which defied the very laws of geometry - under these commander’s instructions, their regiments displayed the 
discipline and skill necessary to survive the most deadly environs. 
AWarlordwiththisWarlordTraitgainstheStubbornandMoveThroughCoverSpecialRules,andinadditionmayignoretheMovementPenalty
imposedbyDebrisTerrain.Theymayalsochoosetore-rollanyfailedDangerousTerrainorHazardousDebristests.IfthisWarlordisamodelwith
theIndependentCharacterSpecialRuleandhasjoinedaunitwiththeTercioSpecialRulethenthesebonusesapplytoallmodelsinthatunit,aswell
astoallmodelswhicharepartofthatTercioandare‘InFormation’withtheunitcontainingtheWarlord.
Inaddition,anarmywhoseWarlordhasthisTraitmaymakeanadditionalReactionduringtheOpposingPlayer’sMovementphaseaslongasthe
Warlordhasnotbeenremovedasacasualty.
SOLARAUXILIAW ARLORDTRAIT:BITTERBLOOD
It is sometimes said that an hour of combat is worth one hundred hours of training; if that axiom held true then some warriors held incalculable skill. Forged 
in the crucible of cramped close-quarters void combat, some even dared to suggest that even the Legiones Astartes might fail to live up to this warrior’s ferocity 
and zeal; inspiring Auxiliaries around them to ever greater acts in the name of mere bloody single-mindedness. 
AWarlordwiththisWarlordTraitincreasestheirWeaponSkillby+1andgainstheHatred(Everything!)andFleet(1)SpecialRule.

designatedthe‘HouseholdRetinue’,andallmodelsinthatunitincreasetheirWeaponSkillCharacteristicby+1,gaintheHatred(Everything!)and
Fleet(1)SpecialRules,andinadditiongaintheLineUnitSub-TypeforaslongastheWarlordispartofthatunit.TheWarlordwiththisTraitmustbe
deployedaspartofthe‘HouseholdRetinue’unitandmaynotvoluntarilyleavethatunitduringplay.
Inaddition,anarmywhoseWarlordhasthisTraitmaymakeanadditionalReactionduringtheOpposingPlayer’sAssaultphaseaslongasthe
Warlordhasnotbeenremovedasacasualty.
SOLARAUXILIAW ARLORDTRAIT:CO-ORDINATEDDEVASTATION
Drilled to a standard higher than even the most elite of Exertus Imperialis regiments, this commander and the Auxiliaries they lead were not merely crack 
shots, but were so well trained that they were able to lay down seemingly unending volleys of fire; even whilst one squad stopped to reload or to cool down their 
barrels, another had already replaced it in the firing line.

COHORT DOCTRINE:PIONEERCOMPANIES
It was the duty of Pioneer Companies to range ahead of a cohort’s main strength; charged with securing and holding forward positions until heavier firepower 
could be brought to bear. Pioneer Sections comprised the mainstay of these pathfinding sub-cohorts and were equipped to demolish static defences and other 
obstacles that could impede the cohort’s momentum. Aethon Heavy Sentinels – piloted by veteran Pioneers – were primarily deployed as part of Pioneer 
Companies, being light enough to undertake reconnaissance duties whilst acting as fire support platforms for infantry. 
Effects

COHORT DOCTRINE:LEGION-SWORN COHORTS
A number of cohorts were raised from the homeworlds and tributaries of the Legiones Astartes. These cohorts usually counted large numbers of failed Legion-
aspirants among their ranks, as well as those who were too old, or otherwise incompatible with the process required to become a Space Marine. Owing to their 
shared origins, these cohorts fought fiercely to live up to the example set by the Astartes. Other cohorts had no lineage with a particular Legion, but had fought 
together across so many battlefields – typically as part of the same Expeditionary Fleet – that their tactics, experiences and losses bound them closer to the 
Legion and its Primarch than the greater Imperium.
Effects
● DetachmentsusingthisCohortDoctrineareclassedasonelevelhigherontheLevelsofAlliancebyasingleSpaceMarinesLegionFaction.
● AnymodelinaDetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayexchangeaLasrifleforaBolterforfree.
● AnymodelinaDetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayexchangeaLaspistolforaBoltPistolfor+2pointspermodel.
Limitations
● ThisCohortDoctrinemayonlybetakenbyanAlliedDetachment.
COHORT DOCTRINE:LINEBREAKERPATTERNCOHORTS
Where the fiercest resistance was met by Cohorts of the Auxilia, Linebreaker Cohorts were summoned to the front lines to resolve the problem. Composed of 
armoured exosuits and dreadnought chassis both, the speartips of these cohorts would rampage forward at speed unmatched by their contemporaries on foot, 
barrelling into their foes and shattering lines of enemy resistance utterly.
Effects
● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayselectSolarAuxiliaFuribundusDreadnoughtTalonsasTroopChoices.
● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayselectSolarAuxiliaExtremisBoardingRigSectionsasTroops.

● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemaynotselectanyFortificationchoices.

COHORT DOCTRINE:APOSTLEPATTERNCOHORTS
Whilst few Traitor cohorts went untouched by the corruption that swept through the Warmaster’s armies, some proved more willing than most to supplicate 
themselves before the Primordial Annihilator. Among the ranks of those who embraced these dark powers, void armour became daubed in bloody scripture, 
officers wielded twisted weapons of unnatural origin and the remains of sacrificed victims hung from vexillas. These cohorts inevitably lost much of their once 
lauded discipline and cohesion – reduced a debased parody of the Solar Auxilia’s legacy – but with damnation came power, as mutation and possession 
turned whole Tercios into monsters. 
Effects
● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayselectPossessedAuxiliaasTroopchoices.
● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayselectMutatedSpawnasFastAttackchoices(seepage227).

maybegiventheCorruptedUnitSub-Typeforacostof+25pointsperunit–modelsthatareattachedtounits,suchasMedicae,mustbe
upgradedseparately.Inaddition,anyunitupgradedinthiswaylosestheClose-OrderUnitSub-typeiftheyhadit.
● AnyCharacterinaDetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemayexchangeaPowerWeaponforaTaintedBlade,TaintedAxeorTaintedMaulfor
+5pointseach.

● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemaynotincludemorethanasingleAuxiliaArtilleryTercio.
● ADetachmentusingthisCohortDoctrinemusthavetheTraitorAllegiance.
COHORT DOCTRINE:ECHOESOF LOSTGLORY
Throughout the Great Crusade, the forces of the Imperium came across untold numbers of human civilisations, all of whom faced Compliance. For those that 
bent the knee to the Golden Throne, their duty was simply to join His forces. Some of these civilisations knew of exotic, arcane, and dark technological secrets; 
and their militaries were thusly equipped. Furnished with these fearsome arms and armaments, these forces became some of the most exceptional members of 
the Excertus Imperialis - and so these remnants fought on for the glory of their new masters, even as they were watched rigorously for any sign they might 
have begun to rethink their oaths of allegiance….

Although among the rarest of doctrines, the sheer scale of the Excertus Imperialis – between the material resources it could call upon and the scope of threats 
its armies confronted – meant that some cohorts were near-wholly committed to the deployment of super-heavy tanks. Not dissimilar to Armoured Fist 
doctrines; these cohorts developed their tactics around the overwhelming firepower and resilience of its super-heavies. Every other element, be it infantry or 
armour, was dedicated to supporting these behemoths as they ground hostiles to pulp beneath their treads and obliterated strongholds with the most powerful 
weaponry in the Solar Auxilia’s arsenal.

COHORT DOCTRINE:VALKYRIAPATTERNCOHORTS
By the conclusion of the Great Crusade, the adoption of gunships and assault carriers among the Solar Auxilia was not especially widespread – with most 
cohorts still relying on the Arvus to shuttle individual sections to-and-from orbit. There were however a number of well-supplied cohorts who took advantage 
of these new aircraft and constructed innovative doctrines to emphasise their use. These gunships rapidly inserted larger numbers of line Tercios directly into 
battle, but remained on station to provide close air support; punching holes in enemy ranks and armour that could have otherwise overwhelmed those on the 
ground.

During the Great Crusade, some Xenos entities proved to be of such terrible power and unspeakable origin that nothing less than their complete and total 
annihilation could be tolerated; the ground on which they had trodden burnt away and salted – assuming it could ever be sanctified for human habitation. 
Even at their peak, there had only been so many Legiones Astartes to engage these threats, and coupled with the sheer number of Expeditionary Fleets active in 
the galaxy, this led to the raising of several Solar Auxilia cohorts trained and equipped for the explicit purpose of scouring whole battle-sectors or even entire 
planets clean of life.

SOLARAUXILIAARMOURED M ARSHALS
Among the ranks of the mortal armies of the Imperium, the thunder of engines and treads often marked the coming of the Solar Auxilia. Within their 
armoured corps, however, there were occasionally stories of individuals - tank commanders - who rose to prominence beyond that expected of their station. 
These experts in armoured warfare were often recognised with the rank of Marshal, rewarded and honoured for their uniquely potent contributions to the way 
the Auxilia waged war.

Among the many Armoured Marshal ranks bestowed, those most commonly seen during the Great Crusade and beyond were known as the Ordnance Corps - 
specialising in the application of immense firepower, trading blows at extreme range with any who dared to challenge them. Broadcasting a command to all 
tanks in range, Ordnance Corps Armoured Marshals could coordinate a creeping barrage that would flush enemy troops from their hidden refuges amongst a 
deadly hail of explosive shells.

## Page 67

Ireton MaSade was a legend 
among the Solar Auxilia. 
Having risen from humble 
origins as a scavenger on 
Terra, he began at the bottom 
as a common-line Auxiliary 
and part of the first muster of 
the Great Crusade. From 
there, his ascension through 
the ranks of the Solar Auxilia 
was meteoric and ultimately 
led to command of an entire 
Expeditionary Fleet. To have 
come from nothing and end 
his service as Imperial 
Commander over the worlds 
of the Agathean Domain, it is 
no surprise that recruiting 
officers held his career up as 
an example all would-be 
soldiers could aspire to in the 
Emperor’s service. 
Nevertheless, Ireton MaSade’s 
tale was largely overshadowed 
by the Legiones Astartes and 
their Primarchs.
By the conclusion of the Great 
Crusade, it was expected that 
MaSade would live out the 
rest of his days in retirement 
on Agathon – his lifespan 
already extended well beyond 
its natural limits by 
rejuvenats and augmetics. 
However, the outbreak of the 
Horus Heresy and the death 
of his beloved granddaughter 
– Jocasta MaSade – at the fall 
of Port Maw would lead 
Ireton to stage a short, bloody 
coup against Agathon’s 
squabbling parliamentarians. 
With Agathon once again 
under the direct control of the 
60th Expeditionary Fleet’s 
‘Old Guard’, Ireton MaSade 
mobilised the Domain for war 
against the Traitors.

Battle Psykers were rare 
individuals possessed of both 
the ability to wield such 
powers and the mental 
fortitude to do so amidst the 
violence of frontline combat. 
Most Battle Psykers were, at 
best, distrusted by their 
comrades, however their 
unparalleled impact upon the 
battlefield usually made them 
respected assets by Auxilia 
officers who wielded them.
Although the Edicts of Nikaea 
rendered judgement on the 
Librarius of the Legiones 
Astartes, some elements of the 
Imperialis Auxilia who 
utilised psykers were 
inevitably impacted, albeit 
indirectly. Several more 
superstitious units quietly had 
their psykers disappeared, 
while others were taken into 
custody, but many 
nonetheless continued to 
utilise their powers on active 
battlefronts where news of the 
Emperor's judgement had yet 
to reach them – or was 
otherwise ignored, if not 
considered irrelevant to 
mortal soldiers.
In the chaos of the Horus 
Heresy some desperate, 
previously disconcerted 
Loyalist cohorts authorised 
Battle Psykers to utilise their 
powers once more, especially 
when their effectiveness 
against malevolent Warp 
horrors had been proven. 
Traitor forces were far less 
hesitant to unleash the full 
might of their own psykers, 
whether they had previously 
served in that role or 
possessed latent potential they 
were now free to wield 
without persecution, albeit at 
far greater risk to themselves 
and their comrades.

## Page 70

Whilst the Dreadnoughts of 
the Legiones Astartes were 
reserved for those warriors 
fallen in battle, allowing the 
slain one last chance at 
fighting to reclaim their 
honour, the Dreadnoughts 
utilised by the Solar Auxilia 
were a very different beast. 
Most often of the Furibundus 
pattern, these gangly, rotund 
machines did not require a 
permanent connection to 
their wearer, instead making 
use of an MIU link to allow a 
pilot to interface with their 
chassis in battle.
A pattern older than even the 
dawning of the Emperor’s 
Imperium, the Furibundus 
made use of colossal claw-
tipped power gloves, varied 
ranged armaments, and an 
unstable yet potent reactor 
core that afforded it a speed 
beyond that of the 
Contemptor Chassis at the 
risk of uncontrollable thermal 
explosions should it be 
breached. Despite these risks 
and shortcomings, however, 
the Solar Auxilia persisted in 
their use; for the carnage that 
could be wrought upon the 
battlefield by a Furibundus in 
the hands of a skilled pilot 
was second to none.

The proliferation of unholy 
rites and worship of dark 
powers spread quickly among 
the mortal auxiliaries of 
Traitor forces, and Solar 
Auxilia cohorts proved no 
exception. Many succumbed 
to the false promises of power 
that daemonic entities 
offered, or else were given over 
to possession in rituals meant 
to forcibly claim the bodies 
and souls of prisoners and 
wounded comrades too weak 
to fend off damnation. Others 
were simply overwhelmed by 
an overabundance of Warp-
energy; rapidly devolving in 
mind and body.
Whether some inkling of 
humanity remained within 
these possessed vessels was 
hard to say, for they displayed 
little willpower beyond the 
compulsion to satiate a 
newfound bloodlust. Possessed 
Auxilia pushed their forms to 
the limits of human 
endurance; surging onwards 
even after suffering blows that 
would have slain a man 
outright. Most retained the 
weapons they carried in life, 
but almost always preferred to 
close in to engage in melee.
Mortal flesh proved extremely 
malleable to the Warp’s touch, 
and as such few Possessed 
Auxilia ever looked alike. 
Many had their bodies 
violently twisted and reshaped 
to become larger, more 
powerful creatures that barely 
resembled anything close to a 
human. While some would 
further mutate into 
abominations known as 
Spawn, most of these meat 
puppets inevitably degraded 
beyond use after a short span 
of time, ultimately casting 
their possessor back into the 
Empyrean.

## Page 71

The Solar Auxilia began as a 
space-bound fighting force, 
and many of the original 
regiments never forgot those 
vaunted origins. In the press 
and claustrophobia of space-
combat, down void-sealed 
corridors and within the 
wrecks of airless hulks was the 
need for the Extremis Rigs 
first conceived.
The earliest Extremis 
Boarding Rigs were ugly, 
brutish affairs; all gangly 
piston-actuated limbs and 
grinding claws to shear 
through bulkheads and hull 
walls alike. In the darkened 
confines of such environments 
did the crewmen of the Rigs 
build their skill, and in time, 
not only could they repair 
their own Rigs in times of 
need, but also assist in ship 
repairs for their commanders 
when required, such was their 
skill. This earned them both 
grudging respect and disdain 
from the Mechanicum; for to 
be utterrly uninitiated in the 
ways of the Machine-God and 
yet able to repair such 
venerable vessels was a 
paradox no amount of 
cogitator stacks among their 
host could resolve.
In time, the Extremis Rigs 
would be codified into a true 
pattern, boasting a range of 
weapons, a gimbal-mounted 
Heavy Lascutter slung over a 
shoulder, and a flexible 
equipment mount to allow for 
servo-arms, vox gear, or any 
other manner of useful tool to 
be harnessed in their 
operations.
138

The hosts of the Excertus 
Imperialis would undoubtedly 
have floundered without the 
support of the Mechanicum, 
owing to the sheer volume 
and diversity of machinery 
and equipment utilised across 
its ranks – from its lowest 
Militia conscript to the loftiest 
Legate Marshal. To this end, 
an array of adepts drawn 
from numerous rungs of the 
Mechanicum accompanied 
the Imperialis Auxilia 
wherever the Imperium sent 
its armies.
Many of these Enginseers were 
deployed to the battlefield 
alongside Solar Auxilia 
Cohorts, usually surrounded 
by a retinue of servo-
automata that assisted them 
in their duties. While the tech-
priests and their guardians 
were known to carry a 
fearsome, often esoteric array 
of weaponry, these disciples of 
the Omnissiah were primarily 
recognised for their ability to 
repair damage inflicted onto 
war machines and static 
defences even under heavy 
fire.

## Page 74

145
Breacher Tercios were 
favoured by cohorts who were 
expected to fight in gruelling 
‘Zone Mortalis’ engagements, 
where even the superior 
training and armament of the 
Solar Auxilia were pushed to 
their limits. The heavier 
arsenal and discipline 
required to maintain the 
integrity of a Breacher 
formation ensured they were 
typically drawn from 
experienced, veteran 
Auxiliaries. 
Although the mortality rate 
among Breacher Tercios could 
be truly appalling – even by 
the standards of the Solar 
Auxilia – serving in such a 
role nonetheless proved 
attractive to Auxiliaries who 
sought advancement through 
the ranks. Leading a forlorn 
hope into certain death 
guaranteed commendations 
and ascension, but survival 
was as much about luck as it 
was any ability to survive. For 
this reason, Troop Masters of 
Breacher Tercios – as well as 
their protectors – tended to be 
younger than their 
contemporaries, though were 
no less battle-hardened for it.

## Page 75

147
Breacher Sections regularly 
served in the vanguard of the 
most perilous frontal assaults 
where other shock troops 
could not be relied upon to 
survive. They were expected to 
advance without hesitation 
and in tight lockstep with 
their comrades to maintain a 
shield-wall against enemy 
fuselages that might 
otherwise break the advance 
of lesser equipped Tercios.
Owing to the close formation 
doctrines of the Solar Auxilia, 
Breacher Sections were as 
suited to deployment on open 
battlefields as they were ‘Zone 
Mortalis’ engagements, 
spearheading the push 
through breached defences 
and down exposed city streets. 
Auxiliaries who made up 
Breacher Sections were drilled 
to be remorseless on the 
advance, instinctively filling 
holes in a line left by a fallen 
comrade to maintain its 
integrity – always denying the 
enemy a gap they could 
exploit.
146

While Infantry Tercios served 
as the backbone of the Solar 
Auxilia cohorts, Infantry 
Support Tercios regularly 
fought in close formation 
alongside the massed ranks of 
Rifle Sections. As per their 
namesake, the sections that 
made up Infantry Support 
Tercios were responsible for 
eliminating threats that could 
withstand even massed lasrifle 
fire, or to pave the way for 
their marching advance – 
greasing the proverbial wheels 
of the Solar Auxilia’s 
mainstay. 
To this end, the Troop 
Masters of Ancillary 
Command Sections were 
responsible for directing their 
subordinate sections in battle; 
whether by following the 
directives of superior officers 
and deploying them 
accordingly, or acting on 
requests for support from 
other Command Sections.

## Page 77

Pioneer Sections were combat 
engineers vital to the 
doctrines of active defence 
that came to define the Solar 
Auxilia. These strategies 
called for the rapid 
fortification of newly seized 
ground in preparation for 
enemy counter-attacks, 
against which their foe would 
break upon their lines to be 
cut down by massed-ranks of 
lasrifle fire.
On many battlefields, Pioneer 
Sections were also called upon 
to range ahead of a Cohort’s 
main thrust; scouting enemy 
positions and, where they 
were able, destroy static 
defences such as minefields, 
bunkers and other obstacles to 
ensure a cohort’s grinding 
advance maintained 
momentum.
Unlike most other Solar 
Auxilia units – who were 
generally equipped in an 
inflexible and universal 
manner – Pioneer Sections 
were known to requisition 
varied armaments and 
equipment, owed to the range 
of roles they fulfilled and 
obstacles they were expected 
to overcome.
150

## Page 78

Special Weapon Sections 
acted as fire-support for 
Infantry Tercios, bringing to 
bear more powerful weaponry 
to eliminate especially 
stubborn or unconventional 
threats that could otherwise 
endure their comrades’ volleys 
of lasrifle fire. It was for this 
reason that with the outbreak 
of the Horus Heresy, Special 
Weapon Sections quickly 
proved invaluable assets to 
commanders faced with 
confronting the Legiones 
Astartes and Mechanicum.
In order to take advantage of 
these powerful but shorter 
ranged armaments, Special 
Weapon Sections were 
typically kept mobile – 
responding rapidly to the 
shifting tides of battle – 
utilised where their massed-
firepower was needed to drive 
back breakthroughs or else 
force open holes in the 
enemy’s defensive lines. 
Special Weapon Sections 
favoured deployment in 
transport vehicles for this 
reason, but could be relied 
upon to march in close-order 
with other Tercios or 
coordinate their fire alongside 
Heavy Weapon Sections to 
hold a defensive position. 
152

Solar Auxilia Cohorts utilised 
Heavy Weapon Sections to 
provide close fire support for 
advancing Infantry Tercios, 
typically in concert other 
supporting elements such as 
Special Weapon Sections and 
Pioneer Sections. While these 
portable crewed-weapons 
were lighter than rapier 
platforms, what Heavy 
Weapon Sections sacrificed in 
firepower they made up for in 
manoeuvrability and tighter 
coordination with other 
infantry elements.
Heavy Weapon Sections were 
equipped with a variety of 
man-portable weapons, all of 
them powerful but requiring a 
short delay for the crews to 
emplace and range in, an 
exercise they were relentlessly 
drilled in again and again, for 
every second counted on the 
battlefields of the Great 
Crusade. Heavy Stubbers 
raked down scores of slavering 
xenos beasts, while mortars 
reigned down from above, 
missile launchers cleared the 
skies and exploded among 
advancing infantry, as 
lascannons obliterated 
armour columns and alien 
monstrosities.
With the outbreak of the 
Horus Heresy and the need 
for weaponry that could 
penetrate the armour of the 
indomitable Legiones 
Astartes, the importance of 
the Heavy Weapon Sections 
was emphasised time and 
again. The lives of whole 
Tercios were spent to buy time 
for crews to reposition, or as 
bait to draw the post-human 
warriors into prepared kill-
zones where only heavy 
weapons were guaranteed to 
destroy them.

The Chimera Armoured 
Infantry Transport was a 
rugged and durable vehicle 
that was beginning to see 
widespread use by the 
Imperialis Auxilia at the of 
the Horus Heresy. Its chassis 
was the basis upon which 
numerous other variants were 
designed, but by far the most 
endearing was its original role 
as an armoured personnel 
carrier.
Many infantry units favoured 
the Chimera not only for the 
impressive protection it 
provided, but also the 
firepower mounted atop it, 
which allowed the Chimera to 
act in a fire-support role after 
its charges had disembarked.
Among the Solar Auxilia 
adoption of the Chimera was 
not as far reaching as some 
other branches of the 
Imperialis Auxilia and many 
of the Cohorts who utilised it 
forwent the lasgun arrays 
popular with regular Army 
regiments. Instead, efforts 
were made to further modify 
and reinforce their Chimeras 
with extensive life support 
systems and superior material 
components, though this was 
far from universal. 
154

FASTATTACK
Commanders and 
coordinators of rapid 
insertion forces, the Drop 
Masters of the Solar Auxilia 
were the cunning and 
cohesive minds behind many 
special operations as shock 
infantry and deep strike 
operatives. Like many of the 
Command Sections of the 
Solar Auxilia, they deployed 
to combat alongside their 
subordinate drop auxilia; 
coming equipped with 
specialist wargear to lead by 
example and action.
The Drop Masters and 
accompanying Veterans were 
the best at their job and duty 
at fighting far behind enemy 
lines, their abilities proven 
through many campaigns; 
successful due to their 
participation, wholly or 
otherwise. Like the famed 
Veletaris, the danger and 
recognition that came from 
such tactics the Verticalis 
employed was much lauded - 
though these back-line 
commanders normally held a 
jaded and hot-tempered 
demeanour, which they 
brought with them as they fell 
feet first into hell.

Some of the most skilled and 
capable of the Solar Auxilia, 
alongside the famed Veletarii 
hosts, the sections of the Drop 
Auxilia were veterans in their 
own right. Trained to utilise 
their Verticalis Drop 
Harnesses to descend and 
deploy into high-priority 
locations and target-rich 
environments, they were a 
contrast to the Veletaris, but 
their job was by no means less 
dangerous. Bringing lighter 
arms, with shorter and 
specialised patterns to lend to 
their lightweight nature, their 
weapons were still capable of 
overwhelming the foe, and 
their discipline as soldiers of 
the Solar Auxilia allowed 
them to perform as dangerous 
and unexpected forces 
dropped directly into the foes' 
flanks.

Whilst the mass-produced 
mechanical walkers of the 
Imperium were generally 
regarded as too weak for 
front-line military service, 
their somewhat lightweight 
build when compared to other 
mechanised forces lent to 
their capabilities in other 
ways of war. 
When fitted with weaponry to 
complement the troops they 
deployed with, and a 
combination of thrusters and 
gravitic plates, they were 
dropped from high or low 
orbit from assault carriers to 
quickly descend alongside the 
rest of their drop forces. Used 
as a highly mobile weapons 
platform to be inserted where 
needed, whether that be in 
response to front-line combat, 
or to aid in the dismantling of 
a key target or objective, their 
weapons provided the heavy 
hitting punch that some 
tactical insertions required to 
complete their battlefield 
objectives.

The Salamander Scout 
Vehicle was a light 
reconnaissance vehicle based 
upon the Chimera chassis, 
which came into service in the 
shadow of its sibling. The 
Salamander took the rugged 
capabilities of the Chimera 
and reduced its size and 
weight, allowing it fulfil the 
role of a low-profile scout 
vehicle and observe enemy 
positions whilst operating at 
the forefront of a battle and 
relay information. 
It was in this capacity that 
the Salamander proved its use 
time and again, harassing the 
lines of the enemy during the 
Age of Darkness.

FASTATTACK
Equipped with enhanced 
augur suites and grand 
auspex arrays, the 
Salamander Command 
Vehicle was a command and 
control vehicle built to rapidly 
receive and relay data and 
form an unbreakable link in 
the chain of battlefield 
communication. 
Frequently fielded by 
Armoured Columns as the 
figurehead of forward-
operating armoured 
reconnaissance operations, 
the Salamander Command 
vehicle, the commander, and 
their learned crew were 
together able to read the flow 
of data through their data 
suites, synchronise 
reinforcements, and stave off 
assaults through the 
disruption of enemy 
communications.
Like its predecessor, Cohorts 
utilising the Salamander 
Command Vehicle refitted 
them to suit the needs of the 
Solar Auxilia, enclosing the 
rear platform, and 
environmentally sealing 
weapon ports, as well as 
outfitting it with further 
visual display systems to 
compensate.
162

Where the punch of a Battle 
Tank was required alongside 
the speed and 
manoeuvrability of a scout 
vehicle, the Pegasus was called 
to the field. One of the last 
Chimera-Chassis vehicles to 
see official construction before 
the outbreak of the Heresy, 
the Pegasus Assault Vehicle 
was utilised amongst 
armoured scouting forces as a 
method of both a rapid 
assault vehicle, and an escort 
to more vulnerable 
reconnaissance assets. 
Building upon the Chimera’s 
capabilities as a Fire Support 
vehicle, using the space 
provided by it’s transport 
compartments to utilise a 
larger engine and heavier 
weapons allowed the Pegasus 
to bring a sizeable amount of 
stopping power to 
Reconnaissance Columns. 
164

Although the Carnodon 
medium battle tank was 
beginning to enter strategic 
reserves at the conclusion of 
the Great Crusade, a number 
of Solar Auxilia cohorts and 
officers still favoured the 
versatility and speed it offered 
over newer, heavier armoured 
designs – or simply hadn’t 
been inundated with sufficient 
numbers to yet retire their 
ageing vehicles. 
Whatever the reason, some 
cohorts continued to organise 
their Carnodon Squadrons 
into Tercio formations, 
relying on numbers and speed 
over technological 
advancements to win the day. 
These Tercios were led into 
battle by officers who had 
spent years or even decades 
within the familiar confines of 
the Carnodon and many were 
stubborn, ageing veterans 
who baulked at the idea of 
mounting any other vehicle.

One of the first forays into 
adaptation of the sentinel 
chassis for front-line conflict, 
the Aethon sported much 
heavier armour than the 
standard Hermes-pattern 
Sentinels without sacrificing 
manoeuvrability. Specialised 
for reconnaissance in 
extremely hostile 
environments, Aethons were 
commonly piloted by pioneer 
veterans which specialised in 
terrain assessment and 
counter ambush tactics.
Up-armoured and armed 
considerably when compared 
to standard sentinel, the 
reinforced chassis of an 
Aethon allowed additional 
racks of heavy weapons to be 
fitted to their frames, allowing 
them to become the dominant 
force in reconnaissance 
firefights among the Auxilia. 
Even when battle was joined 
proper by the rest of their 
companions, their prodigious 
firepower and solid 
manoeuvrability led to many 
a fortified position or 
armoured squadron being cut 
down under the withering 
onslaught of their assault.

Based off of the ubiquitous 
Proteus pattern Land 
Speeders issued to both the 
Imperial Army and the 
Legiones Astartes, the 
Tempestor sub-pattern 
featured a fully enclosed and 
environmentally sealed 
cockpit to fit the Solar 
Auxilia’s exacting needs. 
The Tempestor pattern came 
into being after initial testing 
of it among the Auxilia found 
the Proteus pattern lacking - 
mainly due to the design 
clashing against the Auxilia’s 
particular combat doctrines -
and so work began to adapt 
the anti-grav platforms by the 
Forges of Mars. 
Armoured and equipped in a 
similar way to the more 
common Land Speeder 
patterns, the Tempestor was a 
scouting and reconnaissance 
vehicle and as such found 
itself used mostly by the 
pioneer companies of the 
Auxilia, ranging out alongside 
the Aethon Sentinels and 
other light vehicles before the 
main forces of the Solar 
Auxilia mounted their attack.
Feeding valuable target and 
topography data back to the 
main thrust of the Auxilia’s 
forces, whilst also being 
rugged enough to mount a 
number of heavy weapons, the 
finalised versions of these 
strike craft were well-liked 
and entered into priority 
production alongside other 
staples of the Auxilia’s forces - 
serving with distinction 
throughout the Great 
Crusade. 
166

Fighter saw use among 
almost every arm of the

void and atmospheric fighter 
craft, it could be relied upon 
to swoop into battle, the 
spooling of its Avenger Bolt 
Cannon before it unleashed 
an inevitable hail of explosive 
bolts well-known to spell 
doom for any who dared to 
defy the Emperor’s will in the 
form of the massed Regiments 
of the Solar Auxilia.

Based on the Chimera chassis, 
Hellhound Flame Tanks 
sacrificed transport capacity 
for one of several devastating, 
short-range armaments. The 
most common of these were 
large flamethrowers designed 
to bathe wide areas in their 
lethal payload, flushing out 
entrenched infantry and 
swarming xenos. Less 
common variants mounted 
Melta Cannons to deliver a 
devastating punch through 
the toughest of strongpoints 
and vehicles.
Due to the limited range of 
the Hellhound’s weaponry, as 
well as the volatility of their 
payload, squadrons were kept 
mobile and fluidly redeployed, 
favouring engagements where 
they could rely on speed and 
the density of terrain to cover 
their approach. Many 
infantry sections 
understandably preferred to 
keep a wide berth, owing to 
the risk posed by a 
Hellhound’s destruction.

A relatively recent addition to 
the armories of the Crusade, 
the Kelaino-class of combat 
aircraft was in reality a 
collection of similar vehicles 
pressed into service by the 
forces of the Crusade to 
replace older craft, and to 
phase out craft of a less 
military bent, such as the 
Arvus Lighter. Whilst these 
multitudinous Kelaino 
variants bore many 
similarities to the Arvus in 
terms of capacity, they were 
however a substantial 
improvement in terms of 
firepower, and were capable of 
bearing multiple heavy 
weapons to aid them in 
clearing a landing zone or 
fighting their way through 
hostile airspace.
As the Great Crusade ended, 
and Horus began his terrible 
war upon the Emperor's great 
Imperium, the most common 
variant seen in the Kelaino 
classification would take up a 
name from old Terra's Nordyc 
Enclaves; the Valkyrie, an 
avenging angel upon wings of 
fire and wrath.

heavily-modified variant of 
the Kelaino-class seen above 
the battlefields of the Great 
Crusade and Age of Darkness. 
Stripped of transport capacity 
and capability, the Ocypete 
instead used the internal 
space reclaimed by such 
removal for additional 
munitions and weapons. This 
allowed these strike craft to 
become capable of carrying a 
formidable array of weapons 
that could be customised to 
strike at any enemy target, 
from emplaced infantry to 
vehicle columns, with deadly 
effectiveness.
