The Hades Breaching Drill 
was ostensibly a piece of 
civilian mining equipment 
militarised for use on the 
battlefields of the Great 
Crusade. The Hades Drill was 
equipped with four large 
power-cutters which enabled 
it to easily bore through the 
earth and a set of melta-
cutters to reduce stubborn 
rock to liquid slag. While far 
from subtle, the speed at 
which it could undermine 
enemy defences made it 
popular among specialised 
siege Cohorts.
Unlike its larger and more 
advanced cousin, the Termite, 
Hades Drills were not large 
enough to carry troops 
directly into battle. Instead 
those units assigned to its 
deployment would follow 
directly behind the drill as it 
cleared a path to the surface. 
Those foes who were not 
caught by the drill itself were 
left disorientated by crashing 
waves of flying rock and dust, 
vulnerable to the auxiliaries 
emerging from the ground to 
overwhelm them.

By the ending of the Great 
Crusade, many impressive 
and wondrous technological 
innovations had been 
rediscovered by the 
Expeditionary fleets of the 
nascent Imperium. Among 
these finds were innumerable 
weapons of war; guns, ships, 
and so on - but some 
discoveries were favoured over 
all by the Solar Auxilia - 
Tanks.
Rumoured to have been 
recovered by the hand of the 
Imperial Fist, mighty Dorn 
himself, the Praetorian was 
one such tank - and what a 
tank it was. Boasting 
powerful weapons, a modular 
internal layout to allow either 
a turreted cannon of one of a 
pair of designs, or a centreline 
tank-destroying cannon, the 
Praetorian was swiftly 
incorporated into the 
doctrines of those regiments 
that received them.

Before the age of the Emperor, 
giant steel behemoths ground 
across battlefields of many 
worlds. The majority of these 
vehicles could trace their 
lineage back to old Terra, 
before humanity was 
scattered across the stars. 
Considered obsolete by the 
time of the Great Crusade, a 
mothballing project began, 
especially amongst the 
variants sporting rarer 
armaments; the weapons 
destined to be stripped down 
and refitted onto the new 
Baneblade chassis. 
The outbreak of the Heresy 
put pause to these plans, and 
the forges of many worlds lit 
hot once again as they began 
producing these outdated 
patterns. Millions of these 
esoteric tanks rumbled off of 
production lines, so they could 
be ground to dust in the civil 
war.

A specialised variant of the 
Chimera chassis, Hydra Flak 
Tanks were used as mobile 
anti-air defences for the Solar 
Auxilia, providing cover to 
their ground forces as they 
advanced upon the enemy. 
These batteries were fitted 
with an automated Servo-
Turret, allowing their 
weapons to track even the 
swiftest of foes. This, 
alongside the prodigious 
volume of fire a Hydra could 
unleash, allowed a Hydra to 
rip enemy aircraft out of the 
air with ease.
Effective, resilient and mobile, 
these flak batteries were just 
as likely to see combat on the 
surface of barren airless 
moons harassing void craft as 
they were inside giant city-
sized space stations plucking 
more conventional enemy 
aircraft from the ‘sky’.

HEAVYSUPPORT
A widespread method to field 
plentiful yet capable 
firepower, Static Weapon 
platforms were used by the 
diverse Cohorts of the Solar 
Auxilia as a mundane yet 
robust method of point-
defence, security, and support. 
The most common pattern of 
such platforms was the Sabre 
Weapons Platform, its shared 
parts with the similarly-
ubiquitous Tarantula making 
it appealing to the Auxilia. 
Easy to construct and use, 
static weapons platforms were 
a mainstay of the Imperial 
Armouries; for if not glorious, 
they nevertheless provided a 
flexible means to support 
previously unfulfilled 
battlefield roles and eliminate 
vulnerabilities in a force. 
However, the nature of these 
platforms and their gunners 
left them vulnerable to close 
assault - even so, their crew 
would be commanded to hold 
their ground til their last 
breath, regardless of incoming 
opposition. This, combined 
with the immobility of these 
platforms, often led to the 
attachment to such a weapon 
being considered a martyr’s 
assignment.

Some weapons were not 
merely for the destruction of a 
particular enemy, or a force - 
they were as much about the 
destruction of the very idea of 
resisting the Emperor’s will; of 
crushing all who dared to 
stand before any 
representation of His might. 
To this end, the Deathstrike 
Missile Platform would be 
sanctioned for deployment. 
Carrying immense missiles 
with engines that allowed 
them to cross battlefields as 
easily as continents, 
Deathstrike platforms took 
time to spool up - and so 
commanders would often 
have to spend time protecting 
such an asset as it came up to 
full readiness, before the 
missile committed its stately 
ascent into the skies above 
upon a cone of billowing fire. 
Due to the relative rarity of 
such missiles, the warheads 
available to Solar Auxilia 
commanders often differed. 
Some Deathstrikes carried 
Tactical Nuclear warheads 
whose impacts not only 
devastated the ground, but 
blinded all who gazed upon 
their blooms. Some carried 
Godspear warheads - titan-
killers reviled by the 
Mechanicum and only 
constructed in secret by a 
handful of enclaves on Terra 
itself. The most common such 
warheads, however, were 
Plasma-cored, bursting apart 
with a blast of energy that 
looked for all the world like a 
miniature sun. However, 
rumours spoke of a final 
warhead type, benighted and 
doomed. This was the Vortex - 
and none among any of 
mankind’s armies dared speak 
as to the cataclysm that 
necessitated their original 
construction…

The Manticore was originally 
constructed following the 
recovery of an ancient piece of 
artillery, dating back to the 
murky history of pre-
unification Terra, which 
inspired a regimental engineer 
of the Solar Auxilia to petition 
their attached Mechanicum 
elements to build a weapon of 
similar capability and power. 
The end result was soon 
passed to the requesting 
regiment of the Solar Auxilia, 
and spread rapidly to others 
once news reached them of 
such a weapon.
These vehicles hosted four 
missiles of devastating power, 
mounted on the tried and 
tested Solar Auxilia Chimera 
chassis. Boasting a fully 
automated turret and 
sophisticated range-finding 
and tracking tools, these 
vehicles brought death to their 
enemies no matter the 
environment they found 
themselves in.
Although not as widely 
utilised as the more numerous 
Armoured Batteries in the 
Auxilia’s arsenal due to their 
limited ammunition, 
Manticore Missile Tanks were 
usually held in specialist 
reserve companies and 
deployed when the destructive 
might they wielded was 
required.

ARMIESOFDARKCOMPLIANCE
In the years leading up to the Horus Heresy, hundreds of worlds had been secretly preparing for the onset of civil war and immediately declared for the 
Warmaster. Despite the efforts of Horus’ agents, there were still hundreds more planets whose allegiance remained uncertain or steadfast in their loyalty to the 
Emperor. Taken as a whole these worlds and systems comprised a considerable proportion of the Imperium’s military and material power, meaning their 
capitulation was crucial to the longevity of the Traitor war machine on the Long March towards Terra. The campaigns to control these worlds became known 
as Dark Compliances – a malevolent parody of the Great Crusade’s mission of liberation and enlightenment. 
Even at the height of their strength, there had only been so many Legiones Astartes in the galaxy. Inevitably, the bulk of their number were deployed to the 
most crucial and fiercely contested theatres of the war. As such, the occupation of worlds which had capitulated to Dark Compliance largely fell to armies of 
mortal soldiers who served under the direct command of Traitor Legiones Astartes. Rarely did these officers place any value on life – save perhaps that of their 
Astartes – and no cost was too high if it furthered the Warmaster’s goals. It was a glimpse of Horus’ ultimate vision for the Imperium, where mortals existed as 
little more than fodder to be expended at the uncaring whims of their transhuman overlords. 
As the Horus Heresy ground on, it was the Shattered Legions, Blackshields and other ad-hoc forces who most often clashed with the armies of Dark 
Compliance that typically served as garrisons for important targets within Traitor-held systems. Further armies were, in turn, raised for the purpose of 
pursuing these evasive guerrillas when greater numbers of Traitor Astartes could not be committed. Whether presenting ostensibly easy targets to lure in 
vengeful Loyalists or pinning them down through sheer weight of numbers – quantity, as ever, proved to be its own kind of quality.
ArmiesofDarkCompliancearenotselectedintheusualfashion;insteadofchoosingmodelsfromasingleArmyListasisnormallypermitted,they
areconstructedfromunitsfromseveralsources,andhavetheirownEffectsandLimitationsthatmustbeabidedby.Armiesconstructedinthis
fashionmustbeaPrimaryDetachment,usingtheCrusadeForceOrganisationChart-aPrimaryDetachmentselectedinthisfashionisthen
referredtoasanArmyofDarkComplianceDetachment.

TheWarmaster’sDue
The Traitor Astartes who led Dark Compliances cared little for the mortal soldiery under their command, callously expending their lives in pursuit of the 
Warmaster’s goals.

A number of the Imperium’s worlds were over-abundant in vegetation, or held sparse populations who were required to fight conservatively. Militias raised 
from these planets were often formed around a core of light infantry; sharpshooters and rangers who were trained to outmanoeuvre superior forces.

PROVENANCEOF W AR:VOIDBORNERAIDERS
Some militias, either by virtue of having inherited a system-wide reach from their glory days or else driven by the grim necessity of interstellar piracy, became

valuable than swift mobility.

Individuals classified as 
Ascendant Psykers were 
exceeding rare, even 
compared to the already 
meagre number of psionic 
humans. The ability of 
Imperial authorities to 
control these powerful psykers 
beyond the most direct and 
drastic methods proved 
limited and few were ever 
taken by the Black Ships. 
Mental instability was 
common, though those who 
survived into adulthood were 
capable of defending 
themselves. Many of their ilk 
presented as naturally 
tougher and faster than 
baseline humans, perhaps as a 
quirk of unseen mutation or a 
constant if unwitting 
projection of their powers.
During the Great Crusade, 
numerous wars had been 
waged against those 
Ascendant Psykers who ruled 
entire civilisations as Sorcerer-
Monarchs. In the years before 
the Horus Heresy, several 
forces that would declare for 
the Warmaster took steps to 
protect psykers from the 
Imperial Tithe – in 
anticipation of utilising their 
powers at the onset of civil 
war.
Despite wielding considerable 
power, the consequences of 
losing control for such 
individuals were catastrophic, 
the baleful destruction they 
wrought on others reflected 
back against them. Whether 
out of desperation or 
ignorance, many psykers 
nonetheless pushed their 
abilities well beyond all 
reasonable limits, knowing 
that anything less than 
complete destruction of the 
Loyalist cause would, one way 
or another, lead to their own 
annihilation.
HQ

AscendantRoguePsyker 6 3 3 3 4 3 4 2 8 -
Astropaths were psykers 
trained to send and receive 
messages across the void of 
space, serving the vital 
function of maintaining 
communication between the 
Imperium’s worlds. The 
overwhelming majority of 
psykers within the Imperium 
– whether civilian or military 
– were Astropaths and 
without them the inter-
planetary empire would have 
surely collapsed soon after its 
inception.
All Astropaths underwent a 
ritual known as ‘soul binding’ 
which moulded their psychic 
potential towards their 
defined role, while also 
strengthening their defences 
against the inherent dangers 
of their powers. As a 
consequence of this ritual, 
however, all Astropaths were 
blinded - and many suffered 
additional damage to their 
other senses.
Due to the critical role they 
performed and rarity of their 
kind, it was unusual for 
Astropaths serving as part of 
the Excertus Imperialis to ever 
set foot on the field of battle. 
Nonetheless, desperation 
would sometimes see Militia 
commanders directly assign 
Astropaths to their staff to 
assist with communications, 
or in hopes of utilising their 
powers in an unorthodox 
capacity.

IMPERIALISM ILITIA:
Elites

## Page 106

While one of the missions of 
the Great Crusade had been 
to spread the atheist 
philosophy of Imperial Truth, 
this seemingly-rational 
doctrine would not endure 
beyond it. Agents of the 
Warmaster spent the years 
leading up to the Horus 
Heresy replanting seeds of 
faith among many of the 
armies and planetary 
populations that would later 
declare for the Traitors. With 
the outbreak of civil war, 
numerous cults – usually led 
by firebrand preachers – 
revealed their loyalties and 
proudly declared their 
subservience to both the 
Warmaster and the 
Primordial Annihilator.
As the Horus Heresy 
progressed and Daemonic 
entities tore down the veil of 
reality, many could not deny 
the waking nightmare they 
were trapped in – their belief 
in Imperial Truth shattered by 
the existence of otherworldly 
powers the Imperium had 
declared false. Driven mad by 
this revelation, billions of 
souls gave themselves over to 
what they now believed were 
the true gods of the universe.
The belief that the Emperor of 
Mankind was Himself a god 
was not new, however. Many 
cults arose or returned to 
prominence with declarations 
that the Emperor was a divine 
saviour who would banish all 
evil from beyond the veil. 
Where planetary commanders 
might have once denounced 
those who worshipped Him, 
desperation forced tolerance 
of Demagogues who could 
inspire their forces to stand 
firm in the face of an 
increasingly monstrous 
enemy. 
208

Ratlings were short, keen-eyed 
abhumans, who lived as an 
underclass on several Imperial 
worlds, due not only to their 
barely tolerated status as 
mutants, but also for their 
reputation as indolent and 
duplicitous creatures. 
Unsurprisingly, this ensured 
Ratling enclaves were 
regularly pressed into service 
by desperate Militia 
commanders seeking 
expendable sources of soldiers.
Ratlings lacked the hardy 
constitution of other 
abhuman strains, generally 
making them poor frontline 
combatants – in many ways 
the antithesis of Ogryns – but 
their inherent abilities as 
marksmen and diminutive 
stature led to most Ratling 
units serving in the role of 
snipers and scouts. More than 
once, a battle was decided by 
a Ratling’s well-placed shot 
eliminating an enemy 
commander, though they 
were seldom decorated for it.

## Page 107

The hosts of the Excertus 
Imperialis would undoubtedly 
have floundered without the 
support of the Mechanicum, 
owing to the sheer volume 
and diversity of machinery 
and equipment utilised across 
its ranks – from its lowest 
Militia conscript to the loftiest 
Legate Marshal. To this end, 
an array of adepts drawn 
from numerous rungs of the 
Mechanicum accompanied 
the Imperialis Auxilia 
wherever the Imperium sent 
its armies.
Many of these Enginseers were 
assigned to Militias on newly 
conquered or Compliant 
worlds and took to the 
battlefield alongside them. 
Such adepts were usually 
surrounded by a retinue of 
servo-automata that assisted 
them in their duties. While the 
tech-priests and their 
guardians were known to 
carry a fearsome, often 
esoteric array of weaponry, 
these disciples of the 
Omnissiah were primarily 
recognised for their ability to 
repair damage inflicted onto 
warmachines and static 
defences even under heavy 
fire. 
210

Many of the Kinfolk enclaves 
found on Imperial worlds 
were specialised in mining 
and engineering, whether 
prospecting for the benefit of 
their people or as glorified 
slaves enthralled to Imperial 
masters. Rugged suits of exo-
armour, typically referred to 
as ‘Exo-Drillers’, were 
frequently utilised by Kinfolk 
prospectors to protect their 
crews from environmental 
hazards and hostile predators.
Inevitably, many Exo-Driller 
Teams found themselves 
thrust onto the frontlines 
alongside their fellow Kin, 
where their in-built firepower 
could be readily turned 
against human foes. Already 
experienced in spearheading 
the deep-dives of lucrative but 
perilous expeditions, Exo-
Driller Teams served in the 
vanguard of Kinfolk Militias, 
or outflanked enemy positions 
as part of daring 
subterranean assaults.

GREAT W AR BEASTS
Mauler Hounds, Sabrefangs, 
and Crocodylliads were easily 
recognised as larger and more 
ferocious versions of the 
animals seen accompanying 
human beastmasters. In 
contrast, the scaled and 
winged wyverns were feared 
for their gouts of noxious 
steam that concealed them 
until they had closed in, and 
were often remarked to be 
similar to the drakes of Terran 
myth. Karkinos were another 
seemingly familiar creature to 
most, appearing in aspect like 
the crustaceans of ancient 
Terra; hard-shelled and 
possessed of great armoured 
claws that could shear a man 
in two, should one be so 
unlucky. To round out such 
familiar and yet monstrous 
creatures was the Rhinox - 
shaggy-haired and bad-
tempered, many scholars 
remarked it seemed to be akin 
to a creature that once 
roamed the Nordafrik plains 
where humanity was said to 
have also made their origin.
In contrast to these seemingly 
familiar creatures, Araneae 
Gigantis stimulated the fear 
response of most humans, 
their hairy eight limbs and 
too-many-eyed faces 
activating something primal 
in the mortal mind, for no 
insect of Old Earth had ever 
reached such sizes. Greater 
Annelids scuttled forward on 
many times that number of 
limbs, seemingly ponderous 
until they pounced on their 
prey without warning. 
Similarly possessed of an 
insectoid body-plan were the 
Macroactenicerides, their 
segmented bodies and 
chittering, clicking 
mouthparts belying their 
truth as voracious hunters.
ELITES
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
MaulerHound 8 4 - 5 4 2 4 3 6 5+
On some more primitive 
worlds it was not unheard of 
for Ogryns – themselves 
extremely strong and 
imposing abhumans – to 
successfully wrangle and 
break feral creatures that even 
the most experienced 
beastmasters would balk at 
ever attempting to capture. In 
many cases they were reduced 
to beasts of burden or given 
over to the menageries of 
nobility, but among several 
warlike cultures their fierce 
fighting spirit was retained in 
order to fight their master’s 
foes.
When corralled into battle in 
number, the psychological 
effect of staring down such 
imposing war beasts could 
not be understated, for the 
predators that these Ogryns 
had bound to them were 
always of considerable 
ferocity and tenacity – eager 
to sate themselves on the flesh 
of their handler’s chosen prey.
Unlike the beasts goaded into 
battle by 'mere' human 
handlers, the monstrous 
creatures herded into battle by 
Ogryns were well-feared by all 
not of their oversized 
abhuman strain. No two of 
these hulking beastmasters 
was accompanied by similar 
creatures, each one having 
personally tamed - or more 
accurately, subdued - their 
animalistic charges from 
across the battlefields visited 
by their Militia regiments 
before they were eventually 
called to war. As such, only 
broad classifications could be 
applied such creatures - 
though often these were apt 
enough to indicate their 
nature.
212

The Chimera Armoured 
Infantry Transport was a 
rugged and durable vehicle 
that was beginning to see 
widespread use by the 
Imperialis Auxilia at the time 
of the Horus Heresy. Its 
chassis was the basis upon 
which numerous other 
variants were designed, but by 
far the most endearing was its 
original role as an armoured 
personnel carrier.
Many infantry units favoured 
the Chimera not only for the 
impressive protection it 
provided, but also the 
firepower mounted atop it, 
allowing the Chimera to act 
in a fire-support role after its 
charges had disembarked. In 
addition, most Chimera hulls 
mounted lasgun arrays that 
enabled passengers to fight 
from within the protection of 
its compartment and repel 
attackers from nearly all 
angles.
DEDICATEDTRANSPORT
Armour Transport
CapacityM BS FrontSideRearHP
MilitiaChimeraTransport 14 3 12 10 10 3 14
An incredibly esoteric fighting 
force, ‘Voidjumper’ was a 
designation given to linesmen 
from a myriad of human 
civilisations discovered 
throughout the Great Crusade 
who utilised jetpack 
technologies. Similar in 
effectiveness to jump packs 
utilised by the forces of the 
Legiones Astartes, these packs 
allowed for rapid 
redeployment and were 
predominantly made use of 
for flanking attacks which 
earned these regiments their 
reknown.
Many such forces found 
themselves folded into the 
Excertus Imperialis, with a 
large majority ending up 
seeing combat within the 
Imperial Army itself. Some 
worlds, however, found 
themselves so flush with 
otherwise-rare jetpacks that 
they could outfit even their 
reserve and militia forces with 
them. Though a pale 
imitation of the more 
professional forces with the 
same equipment, these forces 
usually found themselves with 
the advantage of fighting on 
familiar ground, making their 
ambushes and flanking strikes 
all the more deadly.
218

Many of the worlds that 
endured the horrors of Old 
Night relatively intact 
retained the ability to 
manufacture vehicles of 
superior design and 
construction to their 
contemporaries. Of those that 
welcomed the Imperium’s 
coming, or where Compliance 
was achieved without 
extensive damage to 
infrastructure, Militias raised 
from these worlds were able to 
take advantage of their more 
advanced motorpools.
The Rhino was one of the 
most iconic symbols of the 
Great Crusade – specifically in 
transporting the Legiones 
Astartes. Less recognised was 
the personnel carrier’s service 
with certain regiments of the 
Imperial Army, including the 
Imperialis Militia. The 
robustness and reliability of 
its design, as well as the ease 
with which they could be 
maintained and repaired, 
ensured they remained 
popular with the few units 
able to field them.

The Aurox Armoured 
Transport was created in 
response to the growing size 
and staggering diversity of the 
Excertus Imperialis host. As 
more and more worlds were 
brought into Compliance, the 
need to better standardise the 
equipment of the Imperialis 
Auxilia became increasingly 
apparent. Not unlike the 
Legion Rhino, the Aurox-
chassis was intended to be 
easily manufactured on a 
large scale, while allowing for 
various additional weapon 
and armour systems.
As a transport the Aurox saw 
widespread service with most 
branches of the Imperial 
Army, extending down to the 
Imperialis Militia. Although it 
lacked the armament of the 
Chimera and other, more 
heavily personnel carriers, the 
Aurox was nonetheless 
commonly sighted among 
Militia forces – whether 
because they favoured the 
robust and swift design or 
simple necessity.

The Termite Assault Drill was 
a transport vehicle designed 
to burrow beneath the earth 
and emerge behind enemy 
lines, as well as take the fight 
to those rare civilisations – be 
they human or otherwise – 
who lived underground. 
Unlike crude civilian designs 
such as the Hades, Termites 
utilised an array of melta 
cutters linked to phase-shield 
generators to tunnel through 
the ground, rather than brute 
force alone.
Due to its specialist design 
and their disposition for 
fighting defensively, very few 
Militia units counted the 
Termite, or transports like it, 
among their arsenal. Those 
that did had usually retained 
the ability to manufacture 
them from a time before the 
Great Crusade, but had not 
yet committed them 
wholesale to the Imperial 
Tithe by the arrival of the Age 
of Darkness.

The Rhino Advancer was 
originally designed as an 
armoured supply carrier based 
upon the Rhino chassis. These 
carriers were furnished with 
extended hulls and a more 
powerful engine to support a 
generous cargo load.
Although the Advancer was 
not intended to see frontline 
service, a number of Militia 
units on worlds which 
manufactured the Rhino 
chassis – or similar, localised 
variants and derivatives – 
took advantage of the 
expanded cargo space to 
deploy large squads into 
combat.

The Land Raider Proteus saw 
use with a small number of 
Imperialis Militia units raised 
from worlds and civilisations 
which had endured the Age of 
Strife with their culture and 
infrastructure largely intact, 
at least when compared to the 
majority of human 
populations who propagated 
the galaxy. 
Whilst the rediscovery of the 
Land Raider’s STC on Mars 
ultimately led to the vehicle – 
and its numerous variants – 
becoming relatively 
widespread throughout the 
Imperial Host, these were 
almost exclusively assigned to 
the Legiones Astartes and 
certain regular Imperial Army 
regiments. Those counted 
among Militia units were 
nearly always manufactured 
locally, or were models that 
shared only passing 
similarities with the iconic 
design.

Corruption spread rapidly 
among those who embraced 
the Empyreal powers of the 
Warp and for each scrap of 
hellish lore indulged and with 
every drop of blood given, the 
deepening stain on a human’s 
soul inevitably manifested 
upon their body and seeped 
into their mind.
Damnation carried a price 
and many would pay it before 
the Heresy’s end. 
Overwhelmed by the terrible 
powers they had sought to 
control, victims rapidly 
spawned mutations that 
perverted their flesh and 
destroyed their sanity, 
painfully devolving into 
abominations that were 
unrecognisable as human. 
Not one of these Warp-
spawned monsters was alike, 
though all were powerful and 
difficult to destroy – for 
darker forces seemingly 
relished in the agony of their 
creations' existence as much 
as in the horror of their 
victims’ bloody demise.
It was not unheard of for 
especially diabolic cultists to 
willingly bring about such 
horrors; some even sought to 
manifest these ‘blessings’ upon 
themselves. Shepherded like 
animals towards the enemy, 
such monsters proved a 
simple but brutal weapon 
that became increasingly 
common among the most 
corrupted of the Warmaster’s 
armies.

FASTATTACK
Whilst most Militia 
Beastmasters contented 
themselves with the more 
common - and easily 
controlled - of animal species 
found in the wilds of the 
galaxy, some were not so 
easily cowed. To them, the 
challenge of bringing to heel 
an enormous, eight-legged 
and venom-spitting Arachnid 
was the apex of their craft - 
but stranger still creatures 
abounded in the galaxy.
Gigantic Wurms the length of 
a man with razor-sharp 
mouthparts and two-score 
legs, or oversized Crustaceans 
with shearing claws double 
the size of a man’s torso - or 
even the immense beetle-like 
Actenicerides; chittering 
monsters that would pause in 
their attacks only to feed 
upon human corpses with 
proboscis mouthparts, before 
scuttling forth again with a 
screeching cry - all these and 
more were brought to heel 
and forced to fight on both 
sides of the war during the 
Age of Darkness.

Centaurs, and other small 
utility vehicles, were utilised 
across the multitude of 
different worlds for a plethora 
of different roles in the great 
imperial machine. From agri-
world harvesting to rapid 
small-scale cargo 
transportation, they all shared 
incredibly powerful engines of 
varying patterns when 
compared to their overall size.
Such civilian machines would 
not be considered suited for 
military deployment by forces 
in the Great Crusade, but as 
the Imperialis Militia took up 
arms during the civil wars in 
light of Horus’ betrayal, they 
were soon pushed into service. 
Retrofitted with reinforced 
winch cables and light arms, 
these rapid support craft were 
capable of hauling a plethora 
of weapon platforms and 
emplacements to exactly 
where they needed to be.

Based on the Chimera chassis, 
Hellhound Flame Tanks 
sacrificed transport capacity 
for one of several devastating, 
short-range armaments. The 
most common of these were 
large flamethrowers designed 
to bathe wide areas in their 
lethal payload, flushing out 
entrenched infantry and 
swarming xenos. Less 
common variants mounted 
Melta Cannons to deliver a 
devastating punch through 
the toughest of strongpoints 
and vehicles. Militias on 
worlds plagued by orkoid 
fungi and other, similarly 
tenacious organisms were 
usually the first to receive 
cast-off Hellhounds.
Due to the limited range of 
the Hellhound’s weaponry – 
as well as the volatility of 
their payload – squadrons 
were kept mobile and fluidly 
redeployed, favouring 
engagements where they 
could rely on speed and the 
density of terrain to cover 
their approach.
Infantry units were 
understandably wary of the 
risk posed by the Hellhound’s 
volatile payload and more 
than one squad of helpless 
soldiers were reduced to 
screaming torches after being 
caught in a blast-wave of 
promethium expelled from an 
exploding Flame Tank.

troops for an upscaled engine 
and armament, the Pegasus 
Assault Vehicle was but 
another one of many vehicles 
based upon the Chimera 
Chassis. Built to carry the 
Firepower of a battle tank but 
maintain the speed of its 
parent chassis, the Pegasus 
was a lesser-seen but 
nevertheless potent strike 
tank.
Its speed and armament 
allowed it to attack key 
targets, whilst the Auto-
Launcher mounted atop it

assault; all aiding in buying 
themselves time to leave as 
quickly as they struck.

The Salamander Scout 
Vehicle was a light 
reconnaissance vehicle based 
upon the Chimera chassis, 
which came into service in the 
shadow of its sibling chassis. 
Forming a backbone for light 
military vehicles, it was 
known for being rugged, 
lightweight, and easily 
maintainable by the forces of 
the Imperialis Militia. 
Due to the lightweight and 
less-protective nature of the 
changes the Salamander 
vehicle had undergone to 
make it successful at its role of 
reconnaissance, it was 
paramount that these vehicles 
- and the squadrons they 
operated - were kept 
well-fuelled and continuously 
on the move, otherwise the 
vulnerability of its lacklustre 
armour could have spelt 
doom for the crew 
commanding it. Regardless, it 
performed successfully enough 
as a scout to see ongoing use 
in the motor pools of the 
Imperialis Militia. 
232

Kelaino Sky-Talons, by 
comparison to their Militia 
Kelaino cousins, were 
composed of a more consisten 
mixture of civilian and 
industrial aircraft, all of 
which bore large magnetic 
lifting clamps hanging from 
the underside of their fuselage. 
Before the onset of Horus’ 
Heresy, they were largely used 
only as cargo transporters, 
used to haul containers of 
ammunition, resources, or 
scrap; however, with the civil 
war claiming all new 
production capacity, 
Imperialis Forces were left 
with no choice but to press 
these industrial vehicles into 
service, hastily outfitting them 
for use in war. 
On the battlefields, these Sky-
Talons were utilised as a fast 
and efficient way to deliver 
cargo and crew into combat 
zones, performing airlifting 
duties before rapidly 
departing combat airspace. 
This cargo was regularly 
vehicles of the Militia’s motor 
pool, but certain leaders felt it 
necessary to utilise the Sky-
Talon’s capabilities to deploy 
entire retrofitted fortifications 
in the midst of battle, setting 
up firebases and bunkers 
before swiftly making their 
exit.

In the Imperialis Militia, the 
designation of ‘Kelaino’ was 
often applied in a much more 
ad-hoc fashion when 
compared to those squadrons 
of similar vehicles used by the 
other arms of the Imperial 
Army; often cobbled together 
by regiments from civilian 
cargo-haulers and retrofitted 
with weapons. 
Though these refitted Kelaino 
were less reliable than the 
purpose-built versions 
brought to bear by the Solar 
Auxilia, they still fulfilled a 
similar role, ferrying cargo 
and small units to and from 
the battlefield with great 
speed and then providing fire 
support upon arrival.

The lightest class of true 
vehicle brought to bear by 
Militia forces, Achilles-class 
vehicles were often little more 
than civilian cars strapped 
with extra guns. Indeed, many 
were tuned for performance 
and speed over reliability or 
protection, and so it was 
common for this class of 
vehicle to lose as many of its 
number to enemy action as it 
was terrain.
Despite these shortcomings, 
however, Imperialis Militia 
forces continued to field such 
vehicles - for, if nothing else, 
they were cheap, plentiful, 
and easily replaced.

The Carnodon Medium Battle 
Tank saw extensive service 
with most branches of the 
Imperialis Auxilia for the 
duration of the Great Crusade 
and its vaunted legacy would 
go on to inspire the creation 
of several other battle tanks 
such as the Leman Russ.
Built upon the versatile 
modular design of the Aurox-
chassis, its transportation 
capacity was replaced with 
turret and sponson-mounted 
heavy weapons, as well as 
improved armour – all 
without sacrificing its speed.
By the time production of the 
Aurox-chassis had peaked, the 
Leman Russ was entering 
service with regular Imperial 
Army units, inevitably 
relegating most Carnodrons 
and other Aurox variants to 
strategic reserves. As a result, 
the Carnodon was typically 
one of the few battle tanks 
beside the ageing Malcador 
that Militia commanders 
could commit to battle in 
large numbers.

Venator Squadrons were 
found on most Imperial 
colony worlds, being another 
broad class of civilian vehicle 
that could be fitted with 
powerful weapons if the call 
of war was to be sounded.
Whilst a broad classification, 
the most common Venator-
class vehicles were the Tauros 
and Taurox patterns, both 
most commonly seen ferrying 
workmen or Arbites - though 
other patterns were often 
grouped into such a class for 
ease if nothing else. In times 
of war, these vehicles were 
commandeered, and tech-
priests or simply lowly 
maintenance technicians 
would set to with welders and 
rivet guns, attaching armour 
plating, weapons, and any 
other wargear available in 
order to turn these vehicles 
from mere wagons to 
weapons of war.

The Hades Breaching Drill 
was ostensibly a piece of 
civilian mining equipment 
militarised for use on the 
battlefields of the Great 
Crusade. The Hades Drill was 
equipped with four large 
powercutters which enabled it 
to easily bore through the 
earth and a set of melta-
cutters to reduce stubborn 
rock to liquid slag. While far 
from subtle, the speed at 
which it could undermine 
enemy defences made it 
popular among certain 
inventive Militia units.
Unlike its larger and more 
advanced cousin, the Termite, 
Hades Drills were not large 
enough to carry troops 
directly into battle. Instead 
those units assigned to its 
deployment would follow 
directly behind the drill as it 
cleared a path to the surface. 
Those foes who were not 
caught by the drill itself were 
left disorientated by crashing 
waves of flying rock and dust, 
vulnerable to the troops 
emerging from the ground to 
overwhelm them.
240

Static weapon systems 
manned by men rather than 
machine were used all across 
the Imperium, especially in 
the Imperialis Militia, with 
the Sabre platform being the 
most ubiquitous of these. 
Whether industrial and 
purpose-built, or scavenged 
and repurposed, static 
weapon systems were all 
lightweight in structure and 
easy to assemble in the field.
Cheap to create, Imperial 
forces utilised them as a 
means to bring heavy 
weapons to field en-masse - or 
bring support equipment 
which required mounting and 
manning - such as high-
powered spotlights.
HEAVYSUPPORT
M WS BS S T W I A Ld Sv
MilitiaSabrePlatform - 1 3 3 4 2 1 1 6 5+
The term ‘Greater Mutated 
Spawn’ referred to the largest 
and most powerful of the 
many abominations counted 
amongst the Warp Cults and 
Traitor Legiones Astartes. No 
less than enormous, 
murderously insane beasts 
that crushed and devoured all 
before them in a stampede of 
bloody slaughter; it defies all 
reasonable explanations that 
such creatures were once 
human.
How exactly these larger 
abominations were 
propagated proved difficult to 
catalogue, with many reports 
being of questionable 
legibility – their authors since 
driven mad by their 
experiences or discovered to be 
under the thrall of dark 
powers. Some claimed that 
mass sacrifices and rituals 
coalesced the bodies of their 
victims into one enormous 
mass of hulking flesh, while 
others spoke to the seemingly 
random gestation of an 
existing mutant having 
suddenly evolved into an even 
more terrifying monstrosity.
The apparently only 
consistent factor in their 
creation was the 
overabundance of Warp-
energies permeating the site of 
their origin, which mercifully 
ensured these indomitable 
creatures were usually only 
sighted among the most 
corrupted of Traitor forces. 
This would have been small 
comfort to their innumerable 
victims, many of which were 
sacrificed at the altar of a 
Spawn’s creation – if not 
damned to become one 
themselves. 
244

As the Great Crusade brought 
the galaxy to heel, it 
encountered a myriad of 
civilisations. Though the 
technology levels of these 
disparate peoples were vastly 
different, common amongst 
them were the iconic 
Standard Template 
Constructs of old humanity. 
Oddly enough, however, was 
a recurrence of oversized 
heavy battle tanks. Whilst not 
of STC design, these venerable 
vehicles were favoured 
weapons of the tribal 
warlords who had controlled 
Terra before the coming of the 
Emperor, and their designs 
had proven popular amongst 
those who travelled the stars 
in humanity’s earliest days.
Bearing weapons deemed 
esoteric in nature, and 
otherwise far inferior to 
modern Imperial designs, 
these tanks had a poor 
performance record and 
offered horrendous crew 
safety. As such, they were 
deemed fit only for issue to the 
Imperialis Militia forces that 
found themselves drafted into 
a war between transhuman 
demigods - and thus 
desperate for any advantage 
they could claim.

Ancient designs carried 
forward from Old Earth, these 
lumbering behemoths found 
themselves fighting in the 
thick both before, during, and 
after the Great Crusade. 
Up-armoured to the limits of 
the gunsmiths who created 
them, those vehicles classified 
as true heavy tanks were as 
varied as their makers. Prior 
to the coming of the 
Imperium, these vehicles were 
the centrepiece of many a 
Warlord’s collection, with 
which once they would have 
used to level the castles and 
strongholds of other primitive 
techno-barbarians. Since 
those times there had been 
little further standardisation 
of these vehicles; many were 
content simply to leave their 
complex and idiosyncratic 
internal systems well alone.
During the Heresy this was 
deemed a considerable upside, 
as the drafted regiments of the 
Imperialis Militia did not 
need to be trained how to use 
their vehicles anew; riding 
happily to battle within 
machines deemed deathtraps 
in the service of their liege 
lords.
246

An ancient form of self 
propelled artillery based on 
the workshorse Malcador 
chassis, the Minotaur had 
largely lost favour in most 
parts of the Imperial war 
machine; notable exceptions 
could be found in the the 
Imperial Army and 
Mechanicum, both of whom 
appreciated the rugged nature 
and reliability of the chassis. 
This generalised loss of favour 
led to droves of these vehicles 
being mothballed over time, 
with not enough demand 
from those still making use of 
them enough to prevent a 
slow crawl of many of these 
venerable artillery pieces to 
the graveyard. 
With the Age of Darkness’ 
arrival, these vehicles found 
themselves called back to 
service, and crewmen once 
again loaded their twinned 
guns and fired them in anger 
and rage against their hated 
foes.

Slowly being phased out by 
the time of the Great Crusade, 
the Malcador and all vehicles 
which shared its chassis were 
destined to be replaced with 
newer, more specialised 
models of tank such as the 
Leman Russ. By the coming of 
the Heresy, many Malcadors 
were scrapped or left to rot in 
massive tank graveyards as a 
result - and many Militia 
commanders found 
themselves with a fleet of 
these older tanks. With a bit 
of elbow grease and 
persuasion, the most 
functional of these 
condemned vehicles were 
reactivated, before swiftly 
being sequestered away from 
the damage the elements and 
time could inflict, a safety-net 
should their regiment ever be 
called for service. At the 
breaking of the Heresy, all 
available material stockpiles 
were reassessed, and as part of 
that process many Militias 
found themselves overstocked 
with Malcador chassis which, 
despite their efforts, had not 
survived their stint in storage, 
both hull and weapon systems 
damaged almost beyond 
repair.
It was from these broken and 
battered hulls that the 
Defensor pattern was born, 
the primary weapons systems 
of the standard Malcador 
pattern replaced with an 
armoured pulpit, bristling 
with as many fixed-traverse 
heavy bolters as the 
regimental engineers could 
cram on. It was in these jury-
rigged and botched together 
hulls that many militia crews 
fought and died - and yet, the 
effectiveness of such retrofits 
ensured that as time passed, 
many more such vehicles were 
created and pressed to service.
248

Based off of the chassis of the 
robust Chimera, the Hydra 
Anti-Air platform was a 
rudimentary attempt to copy 
the more prevalent and 
effective Hydra Flak Tank 
Batteries fielded by the Solar 
Auxilia for bulk use. These 
copies lacked the enclosed 
turret and servo-tracking 
sub-systems of their more 
advanced brethren; though 
they made up for some of 
these shortfalls by using 
locally-produced variants of 
autocannon with far higher 
fire rates, if greater barrel 
wear. These alterations meant 
that these vehicles required far 
more ammunition and 
servicing than the variants 
found within the more 
vaunted regiments of the 
Excertus Imperialis - though 
were just as deadly to 
anything in flight.

rediscovery by the ending of 
the Great Crusade and the 
dawning of Horus’ Heresy,

Praetorian Heavy Tank 
nonetheless found themselves 
in the hands of scattered 
Imperialis Militia forces.

partially finished and

early production models 
which did not boast the range 
of features that completed 
Praetorians did. As a result, 
for many reasons, these 
vehicles were sometimes 
indistinguishable from the 
more ramshackle, locally-
produced tanks some Milita 
forces had access to.

One of many variants built 
upon the chimera chassis, the 
Helios Ordnance carrier was 
designed as a light artillery 
tank able to provide indirect 
fire support, but also to be 
capable of rapid relocation 
when compared to its larger 
cousins. The weapons 
mounted upon the Helios did 
not match the range of other 
long-firing support platforms, 
but it made up for this with 
the ability to position itself 
efficiently; being able to 
maintain its targeting 
capabilities alongside its 
speed.
Where the Basilisk or Medusa 
tanks of the Solar Auxilia 
would fall behind the advance 
of Imperial forces, outpaced 
by the advance of the line, the 
Helios Carrier was able to 
keep up whilst continuing to 
lay down supporting fire.

Manticore Missile Batteries 
were a common sight across 
the Imperium; fixed artillery 
pieces serviced by a team of 
lightly trained crewmen who 
rain death upon approaching 
enemies. A number of forward 
thinking Imperialis Militia 
commanders began mounting 
these batteries onto the 
workhorse Chimera chassis 
following reports of great 
successes among the Solar 
Auxilia. Though crude and 
requiring a large amount of 
the vehicle's internals to be 
exposed in order to make 
these conversions work, these 
vehicles were undeniably 
effective and remained in use 
among the Militias who built 
them.
Upon discovery of these jury-
rigged chassis, the adepts of 
Mars officially sanctioned 
them and codified both the 
retrofitted variants and 
official constructions as 
Manticore Missile Tanks. 
Though the mechanics and 
commanders who 
commissioned and created 
these vehicles prior to their 
official designation have had 
their names long since lost to 
the march of history, none 
can deny the effectiveness of 
their works.

A term used to describe all 
manner of massive all-terrain 
vehicles of various patterns 
found all across the 
Imperium, Ironcrawlers were 
utilised for a variety of 
transportation roles across the 
galaxy. 
With environmentally-sealed 
cabins to protect the driver 
and cargo alike from 
attackers and environments, 
and an open platform on its 
rear for active security 
personnel, Ironcrawlers were 
one of the Imperium’s 
premium haulers. At the 
outbreak of the civil war, 
commanders recognised the 
absolute size and robustness 
of these machines, using their 
cargo space for transporting 
their troops into battle, and 
outfitting them with a variety 
of weaponry to serve as a true 
mobile fortress.

The very largest of civilian 
road vehicles pressed into 
service by the Imperialis 
Militias of the Age of 
Darkness were classified as 
‘Goliaths’ - and this apt 
classification reflected the size 
and weight of such vehicles. 
Often mounting heavy 
weapons or industrial tools, 
these vehicles could defend 
themselves as capably as most 
Imperial tanks - and with 
their ability to mount crew 
bays, industrial tools, as well 
as a range of various other 
weapons, few doubted their 
effectiveness on the battlefield; 
even despite their decidedly 
ordinary origins.

Whilst the varied forces of the 
Imperialis Militias fielded 
static weapons platforms, 
many self-propelled artillery 
vehicles were also brought to 
bare. Whether retrofitted 
using old or otherwise 
redundant chassis, or purpose 
built through industrial 
processes, all manner of 
artillery weapons were 
mounted for the purposes of 
mobility and relocation, 
quickly proving to be effective. 
Due to the lesser-equipped 
nature of Militia forces, 
Artillery Tanks of a 
less-fortunate nature that 
were found at the whims of 
flanking opposition would 
swiftly find themselves and 
their crew obliterated.
Nevertheless, the availability 
of these vehicles and their 
weapons saw them deployed 
repeatedly and effectively by 
both sides of the galactic civil 
war, often en-masse for 
maximum battlefield effect.

Following the outbreak of the 
Civil War that plagued the 
galaxy from end-to-end, 
forces throughout the 
Imperium retrofitted all sorts 
of large-scale aircraft, from 
their own civilian or 
industrial vehicles to “retired” 
third-line military craft, to 
carry large amounts of 
ordnance for deployment on 
the field as a platform of air 
interdiction. 
The deployment of these 
titanic machines was not 
done without consideration of 
many resources, and the 
logistics required to maintain 
such beasts was only 
achievable by the best leading 
minds.

The Crassus Transport was 
commonly seen in use by the 
armies of the Imperialis 
Militia; more akin to giant 
ark-ships used to transport 
ancient man across the seas of 
Terra than the tracked 
behemoths required to 
traverse the dangerous 
environs of the Age of 
Darkness. The enclosed 
nature of their armoured 
shells offered proof against 
shot and shell to the 
contingent of soldiers that 
huddled inside, resounding as 
though a struck bell when a 
shot rang true. 
Though not as widespread as 
the Gorgons more often seen 
in service of Militia forces, 
these vehicles were prized by 
many commanders on both 
sides of the Heresy.

A designation of locally 
produced siege vehicles, the 
Praetor class was made up of 
a number of specialist missile 
artillery platforms 
encountered during the Great 
Crusade. Much like the 
Macharius pattern, there was 
no standardisation between 
any of these crude, lumbering 
behemoths. 
Generally looked down upon 
by the more regimented 
members of the Exertus 
Imperialis, these vehicles 
generally found themselves 
relegated to garrison duties. 
Though fitted with potent 
weaponry, these vehicles 
usually suffered from 
reliability and maintenance 
issues - though the defender’s 
advantage present to 
Imperialis Militia forces 
alleviated these issues, 
allowing them to bring these 
behemoths devastating might 
to bear against their enemies.

ARCHAEOTECHNOLOGICALRELICS
In the wake of the Age of Strife and the coming of the Great Crusade, considerable amounts of esoteric and powerful technologies were uncovered by the 
Emperor’s armies; whether pried from the bodies of slain warlords or found buried away by unknown hands. These relics included weaponry, as well as other 
dangerous and poorly understood devices that would nonetheless prove especially potent on the battlefield. These were inevitably taken up by the most 
influential of the Imperium’s champions – in spite of the Mechanicum’s efforts to catalogue and hoard them.
Whilst the Legiones Astartes inherently favoured archaeotech that enhanced their killing potential, many officers of the Excertus Imperialis took a view that 
their service was a means to an end; anticipating a life beyond warfare, or at least a dynasty that would continue long after their remains had gone cold. As a 
sign of their prestige and influence, a great deal of such archaeotech made its way into the hands of this burgeoning aristocracy, much of it – on the surface – 
frivolous or relatively impotent - but just as many pieces concealed a deadly purpose or had an unconventional battlefield application. 
ASolarAuxiliaLegateMarshal,RogueTraderMilitant,orImperialisMilitiaForceCommander(whomusteitherbeinaDetachmentwiththe
ArmouryofOldNightProvenance,orhavetheMerchantPrincelingorConnoisseurofAlienCuriosWarlordTraits)maymakeasingleselection
fromthefollowingArchaeotechnologicalRelicslist,payingtherespectivepointscostineachentrytoselectthatwargearitem.Eachitemmaygrant
themodeltobeupgradedwithanumberofnewSpecialRules,abilities,ormaymodifytheupgradedmodel’sUnitType,UnitSub-Type,or
Characteristics.Inallcases,theArchaeotechnologicalRelicwillspecifyallchanges,benefitsandlimitations.
ArchaeotechnologicalRelicsmustbevisuallyrepresentedontheupgradedmodelbyasuitableconversiononthemodelwherepossible.

These automata-constructs contained engrams that made them unusually responsive to esoteric stimuli - causing them to hunt it down with something

and the inevitability of following their path for any similar

of the Great Crusade and Age of Darkness were unkind to those 
unprepared to fight upon them. As a result, rapid innovation was

Anrathe caches to bolster existing supplies. This would result in armour 
wholly unique to that strand of the Aeldari race, designed from the best 
they could muster to defend themselves.

Though famed for their speed, Malcadors frequently found themselves 
involved in prolonged siege warfare where their weaker front armour 
and large size impeded their performance. As such, many Malcadors 
found themselves refitted and up-armoured in prolonged campaigns. 
These siege retrofits greatly increased the tank’s durability - however, 
the added bulk severely taxed their engines, leading to reduced top speed 
and increased rates of engine failure.
